BSC PLM — Implementation Handover

2026-09-28 · @Riddhiman Ghosh · DevX Labs

Purpose and how to use this document

This is the handover to the implementation team for Bombay Shirt Company's product lifecycle tool. Part A describes the process as BSC runs it today, step by step, so the team can check that what it builds aligns with how work actually happens. Part B specifies the target process: every record, screen, step, rule, alert and integration, in enough detail to build from without a further brief.

Audience. The engineers, the product owner and the QA lead building the tool; BSC's functional owners (design, sourcing, planning, production, warehouse, stores) for sign-off on Part A and on the open questions.

Where it comes from. Four discovery sittings, two days on site (27–31 Jul 2026), the sourcing call (6 Aug), the RTW inventory knowledge transfer (12 Aug), the store visit (13–14 Aug), a live read-only session in Enigma (26 Aug), the design discovery call (22 Sep), 22 working files shared by the teams, and the interactive process model reviewed with the sponsor through September. Nothing in Part A is invented; where a fact is unknown the step says so.

How to read.

  • Part A flows are numbered tables: step · who · trigger · data source · fields · format · time · hand-off. Format names the physical thing (a sheet tab, an Enigma screen, an email, a Slack channel, paper). Time is elapsed time as the team estimates it.

  • Part B flows are numbered tables: step · actor · screen and control · inputs · validations · writes · tells · target time. Section 13 consolidates the rules; Section 10 gives the state machines the rules protect.

  • Section 16 maps every current step to its target step so a reviewer can see what changes and what stays.

  • Field names in Part A are quoted as they appear in the sheet header. Field names in Part B are the target record's field names.

On data types, sources and fields. The columns, cadences and enumerations in Part A are what we saw in Aug–Sep 2026. The field lists in Part B are the first cut, mapped to those columns and to Enigma's live schema as read on 26 Aug. Both will be corrected on the Bombay visit and as Enigma API access widens; Section 20 lists what is still open. Build to the record shapes and the state machines; expect field names and enumerations to move.

Out of scope. Costing, margin, working capital, ageing and inventory valuation stay in their sheets. Customer marketing (Superfone), store checklists (OPSR) and any garment visualiser are not part of this build.

Glossary and status vocabularies

The words below are BSC's own; the tool uses them unchanged in lists, on records and in alerts. Every closed set in the second table is the literal TypeScript union or constant list in src/domain/types.ts / src/domain/reference.ts; the build must not paraphrase them.

TermMeaning
MTMMade to measure: the customer buys first, the shirt is made after (about 14 days; ~2 days for domestic fabric). Stock is fabric in metres, held in three buckets: CORE, NON_CORE, BESPOKE. The fabric is the SKU.
RTWReady to wear: made 3–4 months ahead to a forecast; stock is finished pieces by size. The garment (style-colour, per size) is the SKU.
FOBA garment bought finished from a vendor who buys the cloth and makes it. In the model this is sourcingMode = 'fob-nominated' (BSC names the supplier by email) or 'vendor-sources' (the vendor chooses its own cloth). 'bsc-buys' is the third mode: BSC buys the cloth and a CMT factory makes it.
RequirementAnything design asks for. On the Requirements screen a row is one of three kinds: MTM fabric (a Concept), RTW style (a ProductionOrder), or Several options (an OptionRequest).
ConceptThe record of one MTM fabric (kind: 'new-fabric') or construction (kind: 'construction'), from idea to live. Eleven derived gates; six design steps.
Design (on a fabric)One sellable shirt configuration on a fabric (ConceptDesign): product code, working name, final name, style, category Formal/Casual, MRP, cost, PDP copy. One fabric has several.
Product codeBSC's Shopify identifier for a design. The model mints BOM-SHI-FM-nnnn (Formal) or BOM-SHI-CS-nnnn (Casual) from a running sequence starting at 4200. Not the fabric code.
Fabric codeEnigma's identifier for a fabric (e.g. NOMSOXSOLI-1849), minted when design creates the datasheet. The model mints a stand-in (NO + 2 letters of the mill + 6 letters of the name + -16nn) only until Enigma's code is known.
Enigma codeOn an RTW order, enigmaCode is BSC's fabric code in Enigma for the bulk cloth (e.g. FORPDOSOPI-81).
Vendor fabric code / articleThe mill's own article number (vendorFabricCode on an order, vendorCode on a fabric or offer), e.g. Verdi SOK#24594. What goes on the PO.
Swatch bookA physical book of 25×25 cm fabric squares in every store; the MTM shopfront. Each MTM fabric belongs to one book: FORMAL SHIRTS - VOL.1, FORMAL SHIRTS - VOL.2, CASUAL SHIRTS VOL.1, LINEN SHIRTS; bespoke books: Leggiuno Bespoke. Store books come in three kinds (MTM, RTW, BESPOKE), edition AW26.
Block / holdA mill agrees by email, WhatsApp or call to hold cloth for a period with no money paid. Two shapes in the model: a Block record (metres, agreedOn, expiresOn from the vendor's blockWindowDays) and a per-offer holdUntil / blockValidUntil date on a quote.
ExtensionDesign (or planning) asks sourcing to get a hold extended before deciding; sourcing extends the date and sends the options back.
Option (request)One numbered slot on an option request ("Option 1 … Option N"). Sourcing puts ONE live vendor against a slot; design settles each slot on its own.
Offer / quoteA mill's terms against one ask: ₹/m, MOQ, width, lead days, hold-until, composition, note. FabricQuote on an RTW order; ConceptFabricOption on a concept or a request slot.
POPurchase order. Fabric PO to a mill in metres (FabricPo, numbered FPO-71nn; on an RTW order also FPO #24nn); production PO to a factory in pieces (ProductionOrder.poNo, PO #155nn). Raising is not receiving.
Auto-POEnigma's threshold top-up: when a core fabric's on-hand plus incoming falls to the reorder point, a PO for the MOQ (or the multiples needed) goes to the mill. Domestic core: Enigma fires it (source: 'auto'); international: a person raises it (source: 'manual').
Reorder point (ROP)max(lead-time demand, vendor MOQ), where lead-time demand = last-8-week shirts/week × 2.15 m × lead days ÷ 7. The suggested PO is max(MOQ, ceil(shortfall ÷ MOQ) × MOQ).
SaleablestockMetres − reservedMetres − thresholdMetres; Enigma's own formula (physical − accounting − threshold), recomputed by the tool. Store rule of thumb: under 15 m is "Low Stock".
GRNGoods received note. Every inward in the model raises one (GRN-26-nnnn), of one of six kinds: fabric, rtw-po, bespoke-parcel, mtm-round, trims, store-inward.
STOStock transfer order from the hub to one store, sent in waves ("STO Series No."), with a type and a hemming type.
Vehicle roundThe daily vehicle that brings finished MTM pieces from the factories to the unit; each piece is received (RTS scan), short (not on the vehicle) or rejected (turned back with a reason).
RTS"Return to source": the unit's scan that a finished piece is back at the production unit and can be shipped.
PP samplePre-production sample; decision: 'approved' is the PP go-ahead that lets bulk start. resubmit means major comments.
Shipment sampleA concept's second gate after PP: the sample made at scale.
Green slipThe production unit's comment sheet on a sample round (comments[] with area and major flag).
DropA month's RTW launch (Launch.dropMonth, e.g. Oct 26); one launch record per option with six gates.
Display & fit piecesPieces issued to every RTW-holding store at COGS, never sellable: one display piece (shirts size 40, bottoms 32) and a fit set (shirts 38/40/42, bottoms 30/32/34).
Fit sample set (MTM)The three fits (Slim, Standard, Heavy) × four sizes (38, 40, 42, 44) each store holds so the customer can try the fit before the shirt is made.
BillOne customer visit with one or more pieces (billNo, BILL-xxxxx); each piece is its own CustomerOrder.
Order sheetEnigma's MTM sheet: 22 option tiles (SheetOptionKey), 20 measurements (MeasureKey), 4 pattern flags. Every edit after punching is a numbered revision.
PP (MTM stage)"Lying with us": punched, freeze window over, waiting for a factory plan.
Freeze windowOrders punched in the last 2 hours (PP_FREEZE_HOURS, adjustable 0–24) are not planned because stores still change them.
PlanAn MTM production plan (#413nn): one factory, one category, a set of order sheets sent with the fabric. Capacity is a cap; forcing over it is recorded with a name.
HeadroomWhat a factory can take today: perDay − in transit − max(0, WIP − 2×perDay), where perDay is the factory's dailyTarget or its 7-day average out.
AgingAn MTM piece in-factory for more than 5 days.
Extra fabricThe daily "EXTRA FABRIC" email as a record: metres, one of five reasons, one of six panels, against one piece of a bundle. Approval deducts the metres.
CaseA return or alteration raised on one delivered piece (RET-41nn), with a resolution ladder and, for MTM, a factory leg or a hemming-unit leg.
LadderThe order of rungs a store tries: alteration → refit → remake → store-credit → refund; exchange (swap from the shelf, RTW) is a side door.
Hemming unitThe unit that hems bottoms and alters pieces, reached through the warehouse; seven scanned hops there and back.
Store creditA CreditEntry (+ given on a case, − spent on a bill); balance is the sum per customer name.
Write-offA store removes a damaged or unsellable piece from system stock the moment it decides (reason, disposition).
Option (RTW)One style in one colour; sizes are not options. styleOfOption strips the colour after " - ".
Ideal option countPlanning's per-sub-category target (e.g. Casual Solid 35 = 10 core + 25 non-core). A gap against it is what raises an option request. MTM plans around ~30 active options (MTM_ACTIVE_TARGET).
ROS / AWSRate of sale: the model's "true ROS" is the mean of the last 4 in-stock weeks; "top-6 ROS" is the mean of the best 6 in-stock weeks since launch; "naive" is the 12-week mean. The sheets call this AWS.
WOFWeeks of forward cover = stock ÷ true ROS.
Velocity gradeA ≥10/wk · B ≥5 · C ≥2 · D ≥0.1 · E not selling.
OTIFOn time (Dispatch actual ≤ target in-house) and in full (delivered within ±3% of cut or order qty).
TNATime and action: the per-PO milestone calendar generated from the category template (22 possible milestones, day offsets from PO date).
BufferDays between expected in-house and target in-house; ≤ 7 is critical, ≤ 10 at risk.
Mention@person or @role in a comment; addressed to that side on that record until resolved.
Walkthrough modeThe prototype's super-admin switch: every screen and button open whoever you are signed in as; each still says whose step it is.
Quiet modeGuidance off: no explainers, no example strips, full lists.

Teams and systems today

Eight teams touch a product on its way to a customer. Three systems hold transactions; the decisions live in sheets and chat. The model signs in as one of nine roles (Owner, Design, Planning, Sourcing, Production, Warehouse, Store, Finance, Factory merchandiser) with one login per team; colleagues inside a team still appear by name on what they wrote.

Systems.

Enigma (cms.bombayshirts.com; Java/Spring Boot behind a gateway; JSON REST at new-admin.bombayshirts.com; OAuth2 bearer via /oauth/token; a 115-module RBAC layer of which the discovery account held 36) is the transaction system: 8,585 fabrics, 15,826 POs, 195 vendors, 50 stores, 46 warehouses (45 STORE + 1 FACTORY), 47 fabric attribute templates, 59 order states, 318 unreserved orders, ledger ids past 4.88 million. It mints fabric codes, reserves metres on punch, runs the order through production, alteration and returns (/exchnage/*), holds RTW variant stock per warehouse (/design/list-variants-with-inventory), raises auto-POs on 2,311 fabrics (CORE only 111 of 541; NON_CORE 0; 1,762 flagged fabrics have no vendor) in at least two silent batch runs a day (08:00 and 15:00 IST, pseudo-user "Customer", overseerId "1"), and keeps a stock ledger (/inventory/deductionReport) with a running stockBalance. It has no updatedAfter filter, no sandbox, no trims module, no sales/invoice module, no dye lot, no GRN number, no per-roll fabric barcode, and its stored saleable drifts from its own formula.

Shopify and the POS bill the customer, hold customer profiles and sales; remoteId is stored on both sides; attributes map to metafields; 49 of 50 stores carry a Shopify locationId. The Enigma→Shopify stock push is a manual button per warehouse ("Sync Inventory For Custom Design / FOB Design") with Sync Status blank on all 46.

ShipHero holds finished-piece stock and bin locations; shipheroWarehouseId on 15 of 50 stores (discovery had said ~28 locations).

Around them: 22 working files (Data sources inventory), email for approvals and FOB nominations, WhatsApp for blocks, mill stock lists and factory chatter (the daily shipping report), Slack for returns, refunds, swatches and STO follow-ups, OPSR for store checklists, Superfone (replacing MCU) for customer outreach, and Fathom for meeting notes.

The consequence the teams describe themselves: the same product is typed by hand three to five times (fabric ordering sheet, merchandise master, Enigma, Shopify, marketing sheet); five mills' stock lists are re-typed every morning; approvals live in email threads; and no one can see where a thing is without asking someone — usually Janak.

Sources: src/domain/reference.ts (PEOPLE, ROLE_LABEL, MERCHANDISERS, STORES, CORPORATE_FACTORY, URGENT_FACTORY, comments naming Swati, Vishnu, Vishal); src/domain/seed.ts (vendor rows and concept raisedBy names Kunal, Yulette, Janak); docs/Discovery Record — What We Know v1.0 (18 Aug 2026).md; docs/How BSC Works Today v2.0 — Change Record (19 Aug 2026).md; docs/Invisit meeting contexts.md (team table: Janak, Srikant, Parag, Vaishnav, tech team, area managers); docs/Discovery Close-Out — 4-Day Field Plan (07 Sep 2026).md (Parag, Vaishnav, Charanjeet or Sairam); docs/Enigma Answers — Read From Live System (26 Aug 2026).md; data-files/Monthly Drops Products Sample.xlsx (Nitin/Prince); name frequency across docs/*.md (Kunal 141, Charanjeet 206, Yulette 118, Sreekanth 114, Janak 42, Parag 26, Neha 22, Sairam 12, Shravani 5; "Ramesh" does not occur in the docs).

Part A — Current process

A1. MTM fabric, idea to live

Design's job in one line (design team walkthrough, 7 Oct 2026). Design starts with a requirement, helps sourcing close the right fabric, turns that one fabric into one or more sellable designs, supplies the product information, and stays with it until it can be made and launched. Three things are never the same object: the requirement is not the fabric (design states the need, sourcing closes an option); the fabric is not the design (one fabric code carries several designs, each with its own preset, design code, cost, MRP and description, written on the design sheet); and a design that exists is not a design that is live (swatches reach stores, garments are inwarded, and online shoots finish on their own dates). A design carries a temporary name from design until marketing gives the final name and description.

An MTM fabric is "just fabric": the vendor lead time is 2 days for a Bombay mill and about 2 months from China (Sairam, 30 Jul 2026), but the path from the design meeting to a store shelf still crosses 20–30 days of design, tech-pack and costing work before a PO can go out (Sreekanth, 6 Aug), a block that lives in three copies of a sheet with no expiry date, an Enigma datasheet, the fabric ordering sheet, the swatch tracker, the weekly "bible" and the REVISED NAME tab — the same fabric typed 3 to 5 times by different people (Discovery Record v1.2 §01). No single record follows it; the end-to-end duration is not measured anywhere (to confirm — the "8 to 14 weeks" in earlier drafts is an estimate, not a number BSC gave).

flowchart LR
  A[Design meeting<br/>idea, weekly] --> B[Sourcing<br/>mill options by WhatsApp / email]
  B --> C[Design picks<br/>block by email or WhatsApp]
  C --> D[Production unit<br/>sample on the vendor swatch, PAT file]
  D --> E[Design<br/>datasheet in Enigma, code minted]
  E --> F[Design<br/>fabric PO in Enigma + ordering sheet row]
  F --> G[Warehouse<br/>GRN in Enigma, Shravani]
  G --> H[Inward logged, swatches cut<br/>MTM_Swatches tracker, Yusuf]
  H --> I[Sourcing<br/>designs / product codes on the fabric]
  I --> J[Stores<br/>Tuesday Excel, Slack photos]
  J --> K[Design<br/>isOnHold off · Sync Inventory button]
  K --> L[Online ~2 weeks later<br/>swatch shoot, video, post]
#StepWhoTriggerData source todayFields typed / readFormatTimeHand-off

Where it breaks. A1.3 has no expiry column in the block sheet and three copies of it, so holds lapse from memory and suppliers chase Sourcing. A1.5–A1.7 mean one fabric is typed into Enigma and the ordering sheet by the same person, with Fabric Price left blank and TAT at 0. A1.7's PO carries no expected date. A1.9's inward owner is contested (warehouse vs factory) and the tracker copy we hold is empty. A1.10 is typed a third time and renamed a fourth. A1.11 publishes availability a week late, so two stores can sell the last metres of the same fabric. A1.12's Shopify sync is a button nobody logs pressing. A1.13 is after the fact.

A2. Option requests and swatch books

The real origin of most MTM non-core sourcing is a thin range: the swatch-book sheet carries the plan inside its range labels — Houndstooth (6), Non-Core Structures (29), Core Checks (16) — and Kunal's rule is that each range has "an ideal mix of numbers… stripes might be fifteen, checks something else" (27 Aug). Non-core is never replenished; a fabric under the "unhealthy" line (~10 m, set by hand, unchanged for about a year) is the trigger to source a new option before it hits the Threshold and is switched off. There is no request record; the ask is a sentence in a meeting and a WhatsApp of swatch photos.

A3. RTW style, requirement to launch

An RTW style takes 90 to 120 days from the planning number to a store shelf (Neha, 30 Jul 2026: "it could be 90 days, it could be 120"); Sreekanth puts raw material to finished product at ~90 days (6 Aug), and the design team said the bulk is "ready in ~45 days" once the factory has cloth and a go-ahead (22 Sep). Planning works three months ahead (in August it was giving sourcing the November number). The style crosses at least six sheets (Option Count Summary, ROS & Health / the Concise workbook, Merchandise Master, block sheet in three copies, the production tracker, Monthly Drops) and two systems (Enigma, Shopify) that nobody links; the only identifiers that survive end to end are the Enigma design code (BOM-SHI-RE-6797-OLIVE-36) and the Shiphero Style Name.

flowchart TD
  A[Planning · option count vs healthy, +3 buffer] --> B[Design + Sourcing · swatches from vendors, collective pick]
  B --> C[Sourcing · block at the mill by email or WhatsApp]
  C --> D[Design · fabric datasheet and Fabric PO in Enigma]
  D --> E[Planning · size ratio from 3–6 months of sales; total pieces]
  E --> F[Production · factory allocated by email]
  F --> G[Design · style sheet, BOM, job card by hand]
  G --> H[Factory · photo sample, PP sample]
  H --> I{RQC}
  I -->|resubmit| H
  I -->|approved| J[Factory · bulk, ~45 days, tracker re-dated weekly]
  J --> K[Warehouse · inward in Enigma, hemming unit if bottoms]
  K --> L[Planning · Monthly Drops, display and fit allocation, STOs]
  L --> M[Stores · display up, launch, POS + online]

Where it breaks. A3.1 plans from an ideal count, not from velocity or cover: 1,100 units a week × 12 weeks at 80% sell-through needs ~16,500 units, but 136 options × 150 MOQ = 20,400 (× 200 = 27,200), so ~3,900 surplus units are built in before a shirt is designed; only 59 of 134 options were healthy on 12 Aug (79 of 642 SKUs on 02 Aug), and "nowhere is the quantity being addressed" (Charanjeet). The ideal counts themselves are a guess — "whether 25 or 26 options in core is the right number, I don't know" — and two of three option-count rows have a blank ideal. A3.2 is hostage to vendor stock. A3.3's block lives in three sheets with no countdown. A3.4 has no owner everyone agrees on. A3.5 allocates by email with no record, and the size history is already polluted by weeks when sizes were broken. A3.6 is hand-made per style, and trim cards still travel as paper. A3.7–A3.8 keep no version, date or decision; "sample approvals live in email and in people's memory". A3.10 is re-dated by phone. A3.11's invoice goes missing and the hemming unit is invisible. A3.12's display pieces are never confirmed received, and STOs stay open after the goods arrive. A3.13 depends on a manual sync button nobody logs.

Sources: Discovery Record v1.2 §01, §03, §05; Tranbscript of granola recording (Meetings 1, 3); RTW shirts inventory call + transcript (12 Aug 2026); Sourcing call summary + transcript (6 Aug 2026); Transcript of call (Charanjeet, Parag, 26:24–35:49); Discovery 3rd visit; Discovery 4th sitting; Invisit meeting contexts §5–§7; Enigma Answers (26 Aug 2026); memory bsc-design-discovery-2026-09-22 (RTW, FOB, go-live); data-files: Option Count Summary Sample.xlsx, RTW_Shirts_Concise_Updated 10.xlsx (ROS & Health Summary, Line-Item Detail), BSC_ Merchandise Master Bottoms_Jackets.xlsx, Monthly Drops Products Sample.xlsx, Display Fit Sample Allocation Sample.xlsx, Fit Display Projection Jun26 Sample.xlsx, STO Swatches Tracker.xlsx (STO), Trims Working Sample.xlsx, Fabric Trims Deduction Tracker Sample.xlsx; bsc-plm-original/src/domain/reference.ts (OFFSETS, MERCHANDISERS, cited from Production Tracker Final V2.xlsx).

A5. Swatches to stores

Every new MTM fabric becomes 25×25 cm swatches, one set per destination, tracked in a 119-column tab (34 destination column groups) and a Slack channel; the sticker on each swatch is made by hand in Canva / Illustrator, and no store confirms receipt ("nobody knows which swatches reached which store" — Discovery Record §2d).

Who the warehouse is (design team walkthrough, 7 Oct 2026). Two people for now. Shravani receives the cloth, hands it over, ships, and keeps the tracker. Yusuf runs the swatch desk: he gets the design codes and the sticker file once design has created the designs, cuts the 25 × 25 swatches for every store, and sends the photos of what went. The swatches then go to shipping, and shipping updates the tracker.

Where it breaks. A5.4 is manual per fabric and the two accounts of its format disagree. A5.5–A5.6: 34 groups of tracking numbers and no receipt confirmation. Bespoke books reach stores from the mills directly and are counted nowhere.

Sources: STO & Swatches Tracker.xlsx (MTM_Swatches, FOB_Swatches, STO tabs); Granola notes "BSC - Design/Planning" (22 Sep 2026) and memory bsc-design-discovery-2026-09-22; Tranbscript of granola recording (30 Jul); Discovery Record v1.2 §2d; Store Visit Questions (13 Aug) and store visit summaries; Copy NEW Swatch Books Weekly Stock Update.xlsx.

A6. Store day, returns and alterations

A store manager, a master tailor ("the master") and a sales/styling associate run each store across four tools that do not talk to each other: Shopify (billing, customer profile, order entry), Enigma — called Anima on the floor (measurements, styling inputs, production; the name "Anima" does not appear anywhere in the Enigma build read on 26 Aug 2026, so it is a floor name for the same system, to confirm), the OPSR app (daily checklists, morning and end-of-day routines, centrally monitored), and Slack (returns, refunds, escalations, stock asks). There is no CRM; Superfone is being brought in to replace MCU for customer communication. The only view of MTM fabric stock is a shared Excel that merchandising refreshes once a week, usually Tuesday, which stores can read but not edit.

Store manager call, 13 Oct 2026 (recording, AI transcript; timestamps for re-listening). What the floor added to the picture above:

TopicWhat the store does todayWhere
Taking an MTM orderA regular customer with measurements on file orders straight away, promise 10–12 days. A new customer gets a trial shirt first: 10–12 days for the trial, then 10–12 days for the order, about 25 days in all. There is no express service; an urgent order is tagged and made in 7–8 days for a ₹600 charge (other custom brands charge 20–30%).13:01–15:27

What the store asked for: more than one fit profile per customer and a size per product; a "who measured" field; order and logistic comments that reach the factory and shipping; a notification when a fabric is closed; a live view of every store's stock; stickers and price tags printed from the system; measurements that update every open order when they change; a compulsory fabric description at launch; swatches before launch; a WhatsApp link on the website; training in short videos.

Also from the recording (second pass, 14 Oct 2026). Enigma sets the ship-by date once, usually seven days out; when a factory is delayed or shut nothing moves it, so the store cannot tell the customer a new date (12:30). One bill often carries pieces in different sizes, and each piece needs its own size, inseam and fit rather than one size for the cart (19:46, 24:34). Shipping takes a day within Mumbai and two to four days outside Maharashtra (01:16). When a box is scanned in at the store the customer gets a WhatsApp with the website link; several customers' pieces travel in one box to save shipping (01:00). Bills are sometimes split so offers apply across garments, and international shipping is collected through a gift card (18:15, 56:04). The swatch book must be corrected when a fabric changes direction, such as a vertical stripe cut horizontal (01:32). Training for the new system is wanted as short videos (01:33).

How many stores there are depends on who is asked: "around 30" on the floor (store visit, 13–14 Aug 2026); 35 physical stores plus Online, Virtual Stylist and Corporate Orders on the DSR (week ending 30 Aug 2026); 50 store records in Enigma, 45 of them STORE warehouses, ShipHero wired on 15 (live read, 26 Aug); about 7 stores hold RTW shirt stock and about 11 hold bottoms "in 1-1-2-2 pieces" (RTW KT, 12 Aug). The Close-Out plan (7 Sep) lists this as a number to settle because SSSG depends on it.

A6-ii. Selling an RTW piece. One display piece per style, ordered in the size that hangs well (M for shirts, 32 for bottoms — RTW KT); the customer tries the display or a fit sample and asks for a size and colour. Size is checked in Enigma (variant-level stock per warehouse, variantSkuCode such as BOM-SHI-RE-6797-OLIVE-36) and, in the 15 ShipHero stores, in ShipHero. If the size is not on the shelf, the store asks another store on Slack or waits for a pull from the hub; inter-store transfer is discretionary — a manager may refuse to release the display piece, judged on customer profile and product value. Core RTW is restocked from the factory; non-core is seasonal and does not come back. Bottoms are sold semi-stitched/unhemmed (Enigma fabric-master productType HEMMING on 539 rows, SEMI_STITCHED on 595) and finished to the customer's inseam — in store if the master can, otherwise at the hemming unit, a stage no system tracks. The sale is punched in Shopify; Enigma's "POS Orders" module (= "All Preconfirmed Orders") holds the store order.

A6-iii. A customer comes back

#StepWhoData source todayFieldsFormatTime
A6.7The complaint is heard and the ladder is tried: alteration / refit → remake → store credit → cash refundStore managerNo record the store can check: a post in a Slack channel. Enigma does hold an Alterations module, a Trial module and /exchnage/* [sic] endpoints with refundType, refundReason, refundCreatedOn, isReversePickupRequired, restockStatus, restockStoreId, isRestockDeducted, and 16 of the 59 states cover the flow — so the statuses exist in the system but not on the floor; whether stores use them is to confirm. Two statements on MTM: "no MTM returns; alterations offered first where margins allow" (30–31 Jul) and "MTM returns go back to the factory for restocking or adjustment" (13–14 Aug)Free textSlackMinutes to post; days to close — no measure

A6-iv. Stock in and out. Planning reviews ~300 styles across ~10 stock-holding stores every two weeks in a sheet, builds a file and uploads it to create STOs in Enigma; the inventory team opens the STO and ships (RTW KT). Each STO is one line per piece — "1-1-1-1 dalo, then STO create karo" — and the tracker carries an STO type (Display / Off the rack / Replenishment) and a hemming type (Closed hem/finished bottom, Bottom Open/Unhemmed, NA). At the store, goods physically arrive but the STO stays "open" in Enigma; the store cannot bulk-receive or scan a box, so system stock and shelf stock diverge and negative billing follows. Damaged pieces are removed from the floor or sent back informally and never taken out of the system — ghost inventory (Enigma has manual adjustment reasons for it: "Moving to non sellable bin for Display" (19), "…for inventory pull back" (21), but the stock-transfer endpoints were 403 to us, so nothing on STO state has been verified from data — A-25). Pull-back sheets arrive by email when merchandising wants stock back for redistribution, restock or online. Launch information comes as PDFs, specs, colours, sometimes samples and swatches, about a week before go-live, unevenly across stores; display and fit pieces arrive with the drop and are confirmed on Slack, sometimes. Swatch and display dispatch is tracked in a sheet with 34 store columns; no store confirms receipt. NPS is read weekly but the floor calls it unreliable: low response counts, unhappy customers over-represented, stores judged on policy complaints.

Where it breaks. Two stores can sell the last metres at once — whoever finishes Shopify + Enigma first gets the cloth (A6.1, A6.4). Every fabric is typed by name; no fabric-roll barcode. Measurement adjustments by the master are not written down. Returns have no status a store can check even though Enigma carries one (A6.7–A6.11). Store credit lives outside any ledger. The hemming unit is invisible. Receiving is the weakest link: open STOs, no bulk or box-level receiving, ghost stock, pull-backs by email, display pieces nobody pulls when the fabric sells out.

Rules (as practised today). Below 15 m is low; sold-out fabrics are struck off the store's own list; a fabric on hold gets an on-hold sticker in the book; a display piece is not for sale; the first rung is always alteration or refit; only refunds go to finance; a store may keep its display piece rather than transfer it.

Cases (edge cases seen). Fabric shown in the book but unavailable at the mill (bespoke stock reported only as available/unavailable by Thomas Mason and Albini); a customer's measurements exist from another store; an online customer's alteration (destination is the customer, not a store); a non-core style with broken sizes still on display and still on sale online; an STO physically received twice because the first was never closed; a damaged display piece.

Sources: Bombay shirt company store visit summary.md; store visist detailed summaruy.md; Transcript of the store visit.md (13–14 Aug 2026); Store Visit Questions — Floor Level (13 Aug 2026); Discovery Record — What We Know v1.0 (18 Aug 2026) §01, §2d, §3d; Invisit meeting contexts.md (30–31 Jul 2026) Part 1 and §1–2, §5; Discovery 2nd sitting in visit.md; Discovery 3rd visist.md; RTW shirts inventory call.md and transcript (12 Aug 2026); Enigma Answers — Read From Live System (26 Aug 2026); Discovery Close-Out — 4-Day Field Plan (07 Sep 2026) §4; Summary of First call happeneed with them.md; data-files/STO Swatches Tracker.xlsx (STO Type, Hemming type columns as carried in reference.ts); bsc-plm-original/src/domain/reference.ts (order-sheet fields read from cms.bombayshirts.com for BSC-1002370); realanalytics.ts DSR store list.

A7. Replenishment and stock health

Three different replenishment routines run in parallel: Enigma's silent auto-PO for domestic core fabrics (and, per order, for bespoke), a weekly Excel for international core fabrics, and a person scanning every RTW SKU-size by eye. None of them shares a definition of rate of sale.

A7-i. MTM core, domestic. Every core fabric carries a threshold in Enigma "based on two factors — the lead time in transit and the MOQ of the vendor" (Neha, 30 Jul: 100 shirts a week, three days transit → hold 200 m, and "the minute we fall below 200 m an auto PO says dispatch 200 m"). Read live on 26 Aug 2026: isAutoPo is true on 2,311 of 8,585 fabrics — BESPOKE 1,742, BESPOKE_NON_CORE 457, CORE only 111 of 541, FOB 1, NON_CORE 0; 845 of the last 1,000 fabric POs were created by overseerId "1", shown as "Created By: Customer", at 08:00 IST (586), 15:00 IST (199), 02:30 (31) — at least two scheduled runs a day, silent, no approval, no notification; 1,762 of the flagged fabrics have vendorCode: null, so no PO can be raised against them. Two fields are confused in every conversation: the per-fabric per-warehouse Threshold on the inventory row (7 m domestic, 3–5 m for expensive international cloth — "just the manual decision we did a long time back", revisited "once a year", owned by Yulette, "now nobody's really owning it") is the floor below which the fabric is not sold (saleable = physical − accounting − threshold, verified live); the reorder point is a different number that "should move with the season — October, November, December and March, April — but it doesn't, because everything is manual" (Kunal, 27 Aug). Sourcing (Sreekanth) checks every core supplier's stock weekly by phone and tells design when a mill has run out; design then marks the fabric inactive (isOnHold, true on 3,042 fabrics) — after the stock-out, not before.

The workbook's own ROS formula is lifetime: total units sold ÷ (days since launch ÷ 7), with a "demand-censoring fix (09-Jul-26)" for zero-stock SKUsExcel; "all manually made and not 100% reliable" (Charanjeet)Weekly, half a day—
A7.9Decide per stylePlanningWeekly at best; "if I miss ordering something, it will keep selling and stock out"The Concise workbook's nine verbs (Action & Gap Parameters, in priority order): 1 REINTRODUCE — Stock = 0 and Top 6W ROS ≥ 2; 1 REVIEW — Stock = 0, no strong demand; 2 SIZE FILL — Sz OOS ≥ 2, still selling; 3 URGENT REPLENISH — Healthy and WOF ≤ 3 weeks; 4 LIQUIDATE — Velocity E (ROS = 0), Stock > 0; 5 EXIT — WOF ≥ 55 weeks; 6 CLEAR / DISCOUNT — 30 ≤ WOF < 55; 7 MAINTAIN — Healthy, WOF normal; 8 MONITOR — everything else. Velocity Parameters: A ROS ≥ 10 · B 5–10 · C 2–5 · D 0.1–2 · E 0. Stability rule (09-Jul-26): tiers INVEST · WATCH · DIVEST, a SKU cannot jump INVEST ↔ DIVEST in one week. Action History tab: Style Name · Sub-Cat 1 · 28-Jun-26 Action · Tier · 05-Jul-26 Action · Tier · Status · 02-Aug-26 Action · Tier · Status (Confirmed)Verb, tier, status; the 02 Aug refresh: 642 SKUs, 79 Healthy, 563 Unhealthy, 8,048 units in stock, 1,015 sold last week, avg ROS 2.5, peak 6W 43.3, 281 zero-stock SKUs, 420 on EOSS; 271 SIZE FILL; 37 REINTRODUCE forwarded to design ("dependent on whether the similar fabric is available")Excel, built through Claude by Yulette from three manually updated files ("60, 70, 80% confident, not 100%")WeeklyDesign checks fabric availability; EOSS decided style by style ("greater than 180 days old, definitely I have to do something"; "we have kind of stopped [markdowns] — pressure on margin")
A7.10Store replenishment and pull-backPlanning (Charanjeet's team) → WarehouseFortnightly ("I would like to do it every week, but as of now once in two weeks")Replenishment sample: sku_code (BOM-SHI-RE-1132-ECRU-46) · Style description · Size2 · Sub-Category 1 · Sub-Category 2 · Factory (pieces at the unit) · Allocate · Balance · then per store Stock · Allocation · Pullback (Sarath City, Inorbit Mall Hyd, Vasant Kunj, Pali Hill, Whitefield in the sample; ~24 store columns in the live file)"Pali Hill has none, nothing in transit, 20 in the warehouse → allocate two"; an STO per piece typed into Enigma ("1-1-1-1 dalo, then STO create karo"); a pull-back sheet emailed to the storeExcel → Enigma STO~300 styles × 10 stores; a day; 7 stores hold RTW shirt stock, 11 hold bottomsEnigma isAutoReplenishment is false on all 50 stores; allocationRuleId exists but is 403 to us
A7.11Monthly option planningPlanningMonthlyOption Count Summary (see A3.1); Targets & Gap Analysis (Sep-26 to Feb-27 targets 6,148–7,394 units a month against a 4,193 run-rate; 19,082 units in open WIP across 104 POs)Ideal · Healthy · In Production · In Planning (blank, "not tracked") · +3 bufferExcelMonthlyFeeds A3.1

Where it breaks. A7-i is silent, half-configured (430 of 541 core fabrics have auto-PO off; 1,762 flagged fabrics have no vendor) and static; the threshold and the reorder point are two numbers that the same sentence calls "threshold". A7.5's AWS is a typed constant, and the international flag is in people's heads. A7.8's rate of sale falls when sizes break, so "a style that was actually working quietly dies" — the fix everyone asked for is to freeze the ROS measured while the option was whole; the 45/60-day columns in the workbook are flagged as a known limitation. A7.9 has no trigger — a missed row is a stock-out nobody was warned about; the verbs come out of a spreadsheet built by prompting an AI on three hand-updated files, and no reason is captured when a person overrides them. Sell-through cannot be computed because the ordered quantity is not against the option ("we placed 100 pieces and we entered it in the system only … it's not coming easy to us"); the Enigma ledger row (quantity on the orderProductId) is the only place consumption exists. A7.10 is a day of typing per fortnight and never looks at store-level velocity. Making a style inactive after a stock-out, or bringing it back, is decided by eye whenever someone has time.

Sources: Tranbscript of granola recording (Meeting 1 on auto-PO; Meeting 3 on the 90-day trigger, top 6 weeks, sell-through); Enigma Answers (findings 2, 3, 4, 6; stores block); C1a MTM call transcript and Call c1 a call summary (27 Aug 2026 — Kunal on AWS, 12–16 weeks, thresholds 3–5/7 m, approval, no international column); Sourcing call transcript (weekly supplier check, bespoke PO 08:00); Discovery Record §2a, §3a–§3c; RTW shirts inventory call + transcript (healthy definition, 300 × 10, 180 days, 37 options, sources of the AI file); Replishment part by production on inventory.md; Discovery 3rd visit; Discovery Close-Out §4; memory enigma-auto-po-rule; data-files: RTW_Shirts_Concise_Updated 10.xlsx (all eight tabs), ROS Health Report 020826.xlsx, Replenishment Sample.xlsx, Option Count Summary Sample.xlsx.

A8. Warehouse and unit

Shravani's team (she reports to Sreekanth; the "inventory team" Vaishnav leads was named separately on 30 Jul — whether these are one team is to confirm) receives everything (cloth against fabric POs, RTW bulk against design POs, bespoke cut-lengths from mills, finished MTM pieces off the factory vehicle), sends everything (cut bundles to factories, STOs, swatch books, display and fit pieces) and records it in Enigma (46 warehouses: 45 STORE + 1 FACTORY), ShipHero (15 of 50 stores mapped) and one tracker workbook (STO & Swatches Tracker: STO · MTM_Swatches · FOB_Swatches).

Where it breaks. A8.1's reconciliation is by hand and the gap is discovered days later. A8.2 has no GRN number, so the invoice and the receipt cannot be matched; finance's three-way match does not exist. A8.5's status is free text and STOs stay open after arrival ("system stock and physical stock stop matching … negative billing risk"). A8.6 has no receipt. A8.7 is invisible between the mill's email and the GRN. A8.8 is the "five emails a morning" routine. Damaged pieces are "physically sent back or removed, but not cleanly removed from the system"; the hemming unit's in and out are tracked by no system at all.

The five pains the teams named unprompted. Typing the same product three to five times (fabric sheet, merchandise master, Enigma, Shopify, marketing sheet). Not knowing where a piece, a swatch or a trouser at the hemming unit is without asking. Blocks lapsing from memory. Rate of sale lying when sizes break. Returns living in Slack. Behind all five: Janak is the single point of failure for every sheet — "Is this sheet updated?" — and "40% of Janak's work" is maintaining reports.

A10. MTM daily production, today

Every punched shirt goes through the production unit's day: a plan seven days ahead, bundles to the MTM factories (6–7 in scope per the 16 Sep alignment; the working model seeds three — Mahendra, Richman, Harsh), a QR scan at each hop, and a vehicle round back. Enigma already carries the QR (qrCodeUrl on the order line; permissions factoryAssemblyScan, finishingScan, scanAtStore) and 59 order states whose transitions are scoped per factory (BOM / COF / KOR). Most of the numbers below come from the 12 Sep 2026 production walk with Swati, which has no transcript in the docs folder — they are encoded in reference.ts and marked "to confirm"; the "about 40 pieces a day" figure in earlier drafts contradicts the 16 Sep note (capacity example 600/day) and the seeded factory outputs (~500/day) and is not used here.

flowchart LR
  A[Punched<br/>Enigma reserves metres on punch] --> B[PP<br/>~2-hour freeze for store changes]
  B --> C[Planned<br/>FIFO, urgent first, seven days ahead, by hand]
  C --> D[Cut and bundled<br/>QR out of the unit · metres deducted]
  D --> E[In factory<br/>QR in]
  E --> F[Out factory<br/>QR ready, QC at the factory]
  F --> G[RTS<br/>vehicle round, QR back]
  G --> H[Fitting / shipping<br/>to the customer]

A11. RTW production at the factory, today

Once a bulk PO is placed, Parag Mathkari's production team and the factory merchandisers (Nitin Singh, Abhin M, Bhupendra — the tracker's Merchant column; Nitin and Prince also appear on Monthly Drops as dispatch owners) run the order on a WhatsApp thread and one workbook, Production Tracker Final V2.xlsx: 128 columns in six groups (Style Details, Order Qty Break-Up by 20 sizes, Vendor Finalization, Fabric Details, Production Milestones with planned / revised / actual for 22 steps, Final Steps), plus Daily Monitor, Alert Dashboard and OTIF Dashboard tabs and a Config sheet with seven day-offset templates (RTW CMT shirts, RTW FOB shirts, Bottoms / Knitwear / Jackets, Flat Knits T-shirts, Flat Knits Shirts, Jeans, Kurta) and two risk thresholds (critical ≤ 7 buffer days, at-risk ≤ 10). The workbook itself was not in the shared data set; what is known of it is what the prototype's reference file copied from it, and Yulette's "Parag maintains this — his WIP tracker". The 22 milestone columns: Yarn PO · Fabric PO · Yarn ETA · Fabric ETA · Trims ETA · PPS Submission · PPS Approval · Size Set Sub · Size Set Appvl · Knitting Start / End · Interlock Start / End · Cutting Start / End · Stitching Start / End · Finishing Start / End · FI · Dispatch · Inhouse ETA. Parag's own list of what is missing (26 Jul): capacity committed per vendor per category, orders booked versus capacity left per month, a micro-level TNA per PO, sampling and bulk timelines with actuals, and the invoice-to-accounting flow.

A11.8Delivery performancePlanning / ProductionMonthlyThe OTIF Dashboard tab (On Time = Dispatch Actual ≤ Target Inhouse; In Full = Delivered within ±3% of Cut / Order Qty) and the tracker's Planned lead / Actual lead columns; the Fit & Display Projection's PO statusOn time, in full, by factory; Cut qty, Delivered qty, Risk factorExcelMonthlyNever fed back to allocation; vendor judgement "lived in one person's memory"

Where it breaks. A11.1 is paper, so a ratio change means a re-issued job card nobody versions. A11.2 has no arrival record, so a late fabric and a slow factory look the same. A11.3–A11.4 are re-dated by phone; once revised, the original plan date is overwritten and the buffer is computed off a plan that may never have reached the target. A11.5 has no record of what was agreed or who accepted the delay. A11.6's result lives in a message and does not stop a dispatch. A11.7 leaves the PO's ordered quantity and the received quantity in two different sheets. A11.8 is compiled by hand after the fact and does not reach the next allocation.

A12. The analytics the teams run today

Every report is a pivot on an Enigma stock export or a Shopify sales export, typed together by one person and mailed as an attachment. Who builds each one is only partly known: the ageing file carries "AMAN" in its name and Aman is on the C1b RTW call list; Charanjeet (CJ) and Yulette own RTW planning and the "AI file"; Janak owns the MTM merchandise sheets; a "Category Finance" hire under Neha M is listed in the weekly report's own Sheet1. The Product Weekly Report is dated the Sunday its week ends (02/08/26, 09/08/26, 30/08/26); weeks run Monday to Sunday ("15/06-21/06"). The DSR and Stylist KPI workbooks were not in the files shared; their columns below are read from the generated realanalytics.ts (week ending 30 Aug 2026), not from a source file — to confirm against the originals.

Where it breaks. Every number is a pivot on an export, rebuilt by hand. Rate of sale is computed at least four ways — AWS (12 W) and AWS (L4W) on the weekly report, ROS / L4W ROS / Top 6 Weeks ROS on the health sheets, Pre-Sale ROS on the on-sale tab, and the international-reorder Excel — and none excludes weeks a size was out of stock (the Concise sheet has started a "Stock-Constrained?" column by hand). Nine definitions in these reports mean different things to different readers (rate of sale, sell-through, saleable, lead time, option, repeat customer, SSSG comparable, fulfilment, dummy→sellable — Close-Out §3). Reports are attachments, so a store manager reads last Monday's number; tab names drift week to week ("Summary" → "Summery", "RTW DP" → "RTW DP & Chinos"); a hiring list sits inside the sales workbook; the on-sale analysis is on a hidden tab. Nobody can trace a figure back to the rows that made it.

A13. Bespoke, mill to customer

Bespoke is the third made-to-measure bucket: about 1,500 premium options across 5+ mills that BSC does not stock. The mill holds the cloth and cuts a length for each customer order, so whether a fabric can be sold depends entirely on what the mill reported that day. It is about 26% of MTM units (827 of 3,196) and 30–40% of MTM revenue (the two discovery notes disagree on the split).

#StepWhoWhereWhat happens today
1Mill stock reportEach bespoke mill (Leggiuno, Thomas Mason, Albini, Soktas Reid & Taylor, Manhattan Fabriano, Lanificio Cucinello, Lombardo, Alumo, Baruche, V&P)Email or WhatsApp attachmentSent daily or every other day. Thomas Mason and Albini report only available or unavailable, no metres.
2Re-keyingA person in merchandising or sourcing (owner to confirm)Working sheet, then EnigmaThe "five emails a morning" routine: every report is retyped by hand, and the mill's article number is matched to BSC's by VLOOKUP.
3Switching off a sold-out fabricThe same personMaster sheet, Enigma, websiteThe fabric is marked sold out or on hold, which hides it on the website and in stores; the physical swatch gets an "on hold" sticker. Nobody confirms the sticker went on, and nobody measures the gap between the mill selling out and the customer being unable to pick it.
4Customer orderStore associateShopify and Enigma's store ordersThe customer picks from a bespoke book (e.g. Leggiuno Bespoke) and the shirt is punched like any made-to-measure order. Bespoke rows in the fabric ordering sheet carry no BSC fabric code.
5Purchase order to the millEnigma, automaticallyEnigmaEvery bespoke customer order raises its own PO to the mill (the order line shows "PO pending"); the POs go out in silent batches around 08:00 and 15:00. 1,762 of Enigma's auto-PO fabrics have no vendor code to raise a PO against.
6Cut length arrivesWarehouse (Shravani's team)UnitReceived with the other inbound goods and matched to the customer's order by hand.
7ProductionProduction planner, factoryAs section 10Planned and made like any made-to-measure shirt once the cloth is in.
8Bespoke swatch booksThe mills—Books reach stores from the mills directly and are counted nowhere.
9ReportingPlanningProduct Weekly Report, "MTM-Bespoke_Non Bespoke" tabBespoke against core and non-core by ticket band, for each of the last four weeks.

Where it breaks.

  • A customer can order a fabric the mill no longer has, because the sold-out report and the switch-off are two manual steps by one person.

  • The same reports are retyped every morning across 5+ mills.

  • Nobody owns bespoke: sourcing says "there is nothing of sourcing concept for the bespoke".

  • The parcel is matched to the order by hand, and the rate of orders that fail after the customer has paid is not known.

Open questions. Who owns bespoke at BSC; whether the mills would post their own availability; each mill's report format and arrival time; the cut-length commercial terms (per-cut fee, deposit, minimum cut); how often a bespoke order fails after payment and what the customer is told.

Sources: Discovery Record — What We Know v1.0 §2c; Invisit meeting contexts §2 and the pain-point list; Discovery 1 call; Sourcing call transcript 13:22–15:51 (Sreekanth); Transcript of call (Charanjeet, 1,500 options); Enigma Answers — Read From Live System, finding 2; Question Bank — MTM Spine v1.0, block F (Q72–83); Discovery Questions — Sourcing & Procurement, block 5; the bespoke mill tabs in the data sources inventory; section 9 for the unit split.

Part B — Target process

B0. The spine and the principles

Everything design asks for is a requirement, and every requirement moves through the same four stages, whatever kind of thing it is. Stage 3 (finalised and ordered) is what sets everything downstream moving. After stage 3, design decides only three things: the name, the price, and when the piece goes live.

flowchart LR
  A[1 Requirement<br/>design writes the ask] --> B[2 Sourcing finds and blocks<br/>one vendor per ask]
  B --> C[3 Finalised and ordered<br/>PO raised]
  C --> D[4 Downstream<br/>warehouse, production, planning, stores do the work]
  D --> E[Live<br/>stores first, then online]

Principles every flow follows.

  1. One record per thing, and one link opens it from anywhere. That covers a concept, a style, an options request, a customer's piece, a return case, a fabric and a launch. A link that was shared once keeps working even if the page it sits on changes.

  2. Every change of state is written on the record as a dated entry that says who made it. Nobody retypes a status. Where a thing stands, whether it is healthy and whether it is at risk are all worked out from those entries. Nobody edits them by hand.

  3. One team owns each step. The record names that team and tells it through one notification bell. Owner labels always name the team, never "yours".

  4. Enigma stays the master for fabric and design attributes and for the stock ledger. The tool notes that Enigma was set up, by whom and when. It never recreates what Enigma holds.

  5. The store sells by piece. A bill can hold several pieces, and each piece becomes its own order for the factory.

  6. The tool uses the same word for a state that people use (Section 1). Lists show only those words.

  7. Every record is laid out the same way:

    • the headline (where it stands and whose move it is)

    • the step that is open now

    • the other steps, folded away

    • the document that prints from the record

    • files, conversation and activity, when there are any

  8. Buttons say what they do. Forms ask only for what the step needs and keep the rest behind "more". Help text is not part of the product.

  9. Finance is out of scope. Costing, margins and working capital stay in finance's sheets.

  10. Email and Slack are never the record of truth. When a message goes out, it goes from the record and is noted on it.

On data types, sources and fields. The field lists in Section 9 are a first cut. They are mapped to the sheet columns in Section 3 and to what Enigma holds today. The next two steps will correct each field's type, where it comes from (read from Enigma, imported from a sheet, or typed in) and its list of allowed values:

  • the Bombay visit

  • the review of Enigma's connection

Follow the record layouts and the stage sequences in Section 10, and expect some columns to move.

Sources: Discovery Record — What We Know v1.0 (18 Aug 2026); Enigma Answers (26 Aug 2026); BSC design discovery call (22 Sep 2026); the sheets in data-files.

B1. Roles, screens and permission matrix

Nine roles, eighteen navigation entries under seven groups, one bell. A person sees only the screens their team works in (NAV[].roles), and a few entries are hidden from a role that could technically reach them (hideFor) so each team lands on its own list. The Owner sees everything; "Walkthrough mode" (demoAll) makes any login act as owner for the prototype, while every step still says whose it is (ScreenOwner strip: "{Team} work this screen."). A screen a role does not work in is not reachable even by URL — it redirects to the role's home (homeFor = the first NAV entry that lists the role; the fallback is /design).

Navigation, exactly as layout/Shell.tsx defines it (path · label · group · roles · hideFor):

Routes that redirect: /concepts → /design?kind=MTM, /requests → /design?kind=Options, /requirements → /design?kind=RTW (with open= and new= carried over); / → the role's home; any unknown path → /.

Home screen per role (homeFor): owner → Requirements; design → Requirements; planning → Requirements; sourcing → Requirements (but hidden from the sidebar, so sourcing effectively works from Fabric sourcing); production → Swatch desk (hidden) → in practice Factories & capacity; warehouse → Swatch desk; merchandiser → My factory; store → My store; finance → no NAV entry lists finance, so homeFor falls back to /design, which finance is not allowed on (not in the model yet — to build: a Finance home; today finance only appears as a "Pick up · GST & recon" button on the GRN register, and no finance person exists in PEOPLE, so the role cannot be signed in as).

Scope. A store user is scoped to one store (storeId, chosen in the sidebar; STORES has 34). A factory merchandiser is scoped to one MTM factory (factoryId, the vendors with mtm: true other than the hemming unit: Mahendra, Richman, Harsh, Ayaan, TopNotch). Everyone else is scoped to the whole company. Sidebar "My views" saves the current URL (tab and filters) by name.

Read-only screens: What is selling and Delivery performance have no write gates for anyone.

Records a role cannot act on are still readable when linked from one it can: a sourcing user opening a concept sees design's steps folded with an "acts by" line; the store's screen read by another role shows "punches orders, confirms swatches and raises returns here — you are looking at the store's screen read-only".

To confirm: whether planning should keep design's powers on Requirements (the code says yes); whether the warehouse should edit designs on a fabric (the Swatch desk panel allows it); a store-credit ceiling; whether a factory merchandiser may see the customer's name (My factory shows it today); where finance signs in.

B2. Screen specifications

Eighteen navigation entries (seventeen screens; the two vendor pages share one component), the bell (MentionBar, at the top of every screen) and the search palette (⌘K). Every list screen puts its tab and filters in the URL (?tab=, ?open=, ?kind=, ?factory=, ?vendor=, ?slot=), so a saved view is a URL. Lists page at 20 rows (usePager default), most carry an Export button that writes .xlsx of the visible rows, and rows open the record in a right-hand drawer (Drawer) with sections that fold. Outside quiet mode a list shows a worked-example strip (useExamples) with a few rows first. Tab ids are the exact ?tab= values; labels are the exact strings on screen.

B3. Data model

Twenty-six collections on one Db object (src/domain/types.ts), persisted as one document (zustand persist, key bsc-plm-v1, version 96). Every field below is listed with its TypeScript type, whether it is optional (? = optional key; | null = nullable), and where it is born: E read from Enigma today or clearly Enigma's, S seeded from a sheet at go-live, T typed by a person in the tool, C computed by the tool. Dates are ISO strings; money is rupees as number; metres are number with two decimals (round2). Only ProductionOrder carries an activity[] log; other records carry their history in typed sub-objects (revisions, scans, attempts, nameHistory) or on the thread. Prototype identifiers are minted from Date.now().toString(36); the human numbers are sequential (REQ-nnn, FPO-71nn, PO #155nn, BSC-731nn, RET-41nn, GRN-26-nnnn, #413nn).

Db collections: people, vendors, orders, escalations, options, fabrics, ranges, blocks, stores, swatchDispatches, transfers, returns, threads, customerOrders, stockRequests, launches, plans, extraFabric, factoryOutputs, swatchBooks, fabricPos, trims, concepts, attachments, grns, fitSamples, optionRequests?, writeOffs, creditLedger?.

Person — id: string · name: string · role: RoleId · title: string (all S/T; one login per team).

Vendor (factories, mills, hemming unit, trims) — id: string · name: string E · kind: VendorKind T · location: string S (tracker "Factory location") · region: string S · leadDays: number C/T (measured from past POs; editable) · onTimeRate: number 0..1 C · moq: number T · blockWindowDays: number T (mills only) · mtm?: boolean T · tailors?: number T · distanceKm?: number T · dailyOut?: number[] (last 7 days) T/E (the WhatsApp shipping report) · dailyTarget?: number T · corporate?: boolean T (only one vendor at a time) · urgentReserve?: boolean T (only one at a time). Derived: vendorStatsOf (measured lead, OTIF %, avg slip, quotes won, grade Reliable/Watch/Poor/Too new), factoryLoadOf (wip, inTransit, ready, outToday, avgOut, perDay, nominal, aging, overdue, headroom, load).

ProductionOrder (an RTW or FOB style: requirement → bulk PO) — id · poNo: string | null C (minted on allocation). Design writes: category: GarmentCategory T · subCategory: string T · styleName: string T (sheet "Style / Color") · shipheroName: string | null S · program: string | null S · launchMonth: string | null T · fit: string | null T · orderQty: number T · sizeBreakup: { size: string; qty: number }[] C then T · targetInhouse: string T · targetCost: number T · styleHandover: string T · fabricNominated: boolean T · sourcingMode?: SourcingMode T (default fabricNominated ? 'bsc-buys' : 'vendor-sources') · fobNomination?: FobNomination | null = { supplierVendorId: string; emailedOn: string | null; by: string | null; note: string } T · fabricBrief: FabricBrief | null = { look: string; composition: string | null; colour: string | null; weightGsm: number | null; referenceFabricId: string | null; notes: string } T · bom: BomLine[] C (defaultBom) then T.
