Grn — { id; grnNo: string C (GRN-26-nnnn); kind: GrnKind; ref: string C (PO no., parcel code, "round · Mahendra"); refLabel: string C; vendorId: string | null; storeId?: string | null; receivedOn C; by C; qty: number; unit: 'm' | 'pc'; rejected: number; invoiceNo: string | null T; poNo: string | null C; financeSeenOn: string | null C; remarks: string }.

Thread { id; kind: ThreadKind; subjectId: string; comments: Comment[] }; Comment { id; at; who; role: RoleId; text: string; step: string | null; mentions: string[] (person ids and/or role ids, parsed from @); resolved?: { by; at }[] } T/C. Attachment { id; kind: AttachmentKind; subjectId; label: string; step: string | null; name: string; size: number; mime: string; by; at; stored: boolean } T (bytes in IndexedDB in the prototype).

Keys and identity. Fabric code and order number are Enigma's; product code (BOM-SHI-…) is BSC's and is what Shopify sells; bill number, case number, GRN number, request number and plan number are the tool's. The tool must never invent a fabric code for Enigma — the stand-in NOxx…-16nn marks a cloth Enigma has not yet minted. Computed fields are never stored: requirement stage, sourcing state word, concept stage and gates, design steps, health verb, fabric live status, ROP, milestone risk, OTIF, launch stage, return status label, sheet edit mode, credit balance, headroom.

Enumerations to mirror from Enigma (not the tool's own): inventoryType (CORE, NON_CORE, BESPOKE, BESPOKE_NON_CORE, SKU, FOB, ACCESSORIES, EXTERNAL_MANUFACTURER, FREE), productType (CUSTOM_DESIGN, READY_TO_WEAR, SEMI_STITCHED, HEMMING, READY_DESIGN), PO status (DRAFT, Open, Cancelled, Close), the 59 order states, the 22 stock-adjustment reasons.

To confirm: product-code format; whether size sets come from Enigma's Sizing module or SIZE_SETS; whether consumptionM is read from the ledger row or the order sheet; the pause-reason list (free text today); whether Vendor.leadDays is promised or measured.

Sources: src/domain/types.ts (every interface, field by field); src/store.ts (which action writes each field; minted numbers; persist version); src/domain/engines.ts (the derived functions named); src/domain/reference.ts (STORES, TRIM_MASTER, SIZE_SETS); docs/Enigma Answers — Read From Live System (26 Aug 2026).md (Enigma enumerations and which fields are Enigma's).

B4. State machines

Each record's stage is derived from these transitions; the actions in store.ts are the only ways to move, and the guards in B13 decide whether a move is written. A transition not listed is not offered.

Sourcing on an RTW/FOB style (ProductionOrder.status × sourcing.stage).

  • Requirement → Quoting (vendors): handToSourcing (design/planning) or the first addFabricQuote.

  • vendors → design-choice: submitToDesign (needs ≥ 1 quote). A second addFabricQuote replaces the unchosen quote ("Vendor replaced · … withdrawn by sourcing").

  • design-choice → extension: askExtension (design or planning; the team that clicked is logged).

  • extension → design-choice: extendQuote then sendBackToDesign (marks the extension resolved).

  • design-choice | extension → vendors: rejectFabricQuote or removeFabricQuote on the last quote (stage, submittedOn and extension reset).

  • design-choice → chosen and status Allocated: chooseFabricVendor (sets fabricVendorId, fabricMetres, costing.cpm).

  • chosen → design-choice and status Quoting: unchooseFabricVendor (only while no fabric PO).

  • chosen → po-raised: raiseOrderFabricPo; or design-choice → po-raised and Allocated in one click: acceptQuoteAndRaisePo (Push PO). Both mint the fabric row on the master, a FabricPo, and land the "Fabric PO" milestone.

  • Allocated → Open: allocateFactory (planning; refused while a nominated fabric has no PO) — mints poNo, poDate, merchant, TNA, materials.

  • Open → Closed: receiveLot when the balance reaches zero or closeShort; legacy receive.

  • Cancelled: no action sets it (seed only).

  • FOB: setSourcingMode flips fabricNominated; markFobEmailed stamps the nomination. The "Vendors" step is done when the nomination is emailed; no PO step.

Sourcing on a concept (Concept.fabricOptions, optionsSentOn, fabricId, blockId, fabricPo).

  • add vendor (conceptAddFabricOption, replaces the live one; refused after a PO) → conceptSendOptionsToDesign sets optionsSentOn.

  • design: conceptAcceptAndRaisePo (Push PO — selects, mints the fabric on the master if needed, raises the FabricPo, sets fabricPo); conceptRejectFabricOption (last live one clears optionsSentOn → back to sourcing); conceptAskOptionExtension → sourcing conceptExtendOption → conceptSendOptionsBack; sourcing conceptRemoveFabricOption (withdraw).

  • older path still in the store, no button: conceptChooseFabric, conceptChooseFabricOption (choose without PO) → conceptBlockFabric (Vendor has blocked it, creates a Block held for the vendor's window) → conceptRaiseFabricPo (converts the block); conceptUnchooseFabric only while no block and no PO.

  • concept lifecycle: conceptSendPat → conceptSubmitSample('pp') → conceptReviewSample (resubmit = another round; approved) → conceptSubmitSample('shipment') → review → conceptSendApproval (posts the @planning @production @warehouse @store comment) → priceFabric → Enigma (conceptConfigureEnigma, conceptSetEnigmaFlags) → designs (conceptAddDesign / update / flags / remove) → conceptSetName → conceptSetMarketing → conceptGoLive (sets liveOn, clears the fabric's onHold) → online steps. conceptGoLive is not gated on the swatch desk; "Live" on the Requirements list additionally needs online.liveOn.

Option slot (RequestOption.status).

  • open → (sourcing addRequestOffer, replaces the live vendor) → with-design via sendOptionToDesign.

  • with-design → po-raised: acceptOfferAndRaisePo (mints the fabric on the master with onHold: true, the FabricPo, and a new-fabric Concept with approvalSentOn set so the swatch desk picks it up).

  • with-design → extension: askOptionExtension; extension → with-design: extendRequestOffer + sendOptionBack.

  • with-design | extension → open: rejectRequestOffer on the last live offer, or removeRequestOffer (sourcing withdraws).

  • any except po-raised → closed: closeOption (reason); closed → with-design or open: reopenOption.

  • Request stage on the list: 3 once every live slot has its PO; 2 while any slot is open / with design / extension / done; 1 otherwise.

Requirement stage (computed on the Requirements list). RTW style: 1 while status = Requirement; 2 until the fabric PO exists (or, for FOB / vendor-sources, until the nomination / no PO needed); 3 once poDone; 4 once production has begun (fabric ready, a material received, a factory allocated, or a sample); 5 (Live) once its launch is launched. MTM concept: 1 until an offer, optionsSentOn or approval; 2 until the fabric PO; 3 after the PO; 4 once the swatch desk has an inward date or liveOn; 5 once live in stores and online.

Vendor block. held → converted (convertBlock, or automatically by conceptRaiseFabricPo) · held → released (releaseBlock). expired is in the type but nothing sets it: the countdown simply reads "lapsed nd ago" (not in the model yet — to build: a nightly job). A lapsed hold does not block Push PO in the model; the alert says "the mill's hold lapsed nd ago — ask sourcing to extend" (blocking is to build).

Fabric (MTM master). Live status = Paused if paused · On Hold if onHold · BESPOKE: Sold Out if millAvailable === false else Active · Sold Out if saleable ≤ 0 · Low Stock if saleable < 15 m · Active. Moves: pauseFabric / resumeFabric (design); setFabricHold (sourcing, and automatically by reportMillStock, saveMillStock, importMillSheet when the mill says out, and cleared by conceptGoLive); setFabricBucket NON_CORE → CORE (threshold raised to 40 if under 20, promotedOn, core block cleared) or CORE → NON_CORE; BESPOKE never changes bucket (the screen hides the control); agreeCoreBlock / endCoreBlock; renameFabric appends to nameHistory; reviewCoreStock writes supply; raiseFabricPo adds incomingMetres; receiveFabricPo / receiveFabricGrn move metres from incoming to stock and raise a GRN.

Fabric PO. raised → (confirmFabricPo sets vendorConfirmedOn / expectedOn / remark; remarkFabricPo) → receiveFabricPo (received, GRN). Status word computed: awaiting confirmation → unconfirmed · chase after 2 days → confirmed → confirmed · overdue → received.

Customer order (one piece) (CustomerOrder.stage, moved only by scanOrders, submitPlan, unplanOrders, receiveRound, inwardBespoke, fulfilOrder, storeInward, cancelOrder).

  • MTM: punched (reserves metres) → pp (the 2-hour freeze is computed, not a stored stage; inwardBespoke also moves punched → pp) → planned (submitPlan; unplanOrders goes back to pp and frees the factory) → in-transit (scan "Out of production unit": metres deducted from stock and reservation) → in-factory (scan) → out-factory (scan; factory or production) → rts (vehicle round / RTS scan, raises the mtm-round GRN) → dispatched (scan) → delivered (store counts it in, or fulfilOrder). receiveRound sends a rejected piece back to in-factory with the reason on receiving; a short piece stays out-factory with shortOn. cancelOrder from any stage → cancelled (reservation released only before the cloth was cut).

  • RTW: punched → dispatched (fulfilOrder from the hub) → delivered; or shelf hand-over via fulfilOrder. The store's advanceOrder (punched → ready → delivered, ready → hemming when an inseam is set) exists in the store but no button calls it (not in the model yet — to build the hemming bench's own scan).

  • Sheet edits (sheetEditMode): unit — free in punched / pp / planned, amend (revision flagged reprint, cleared by reissueSheet) in in-transit / in-factory, closed after; store — free in punched / pp while no factory is allocated, remarks in punched / pp / planned / in-transit / in-factory, closed after. Bespoke: callBespokeFabric sets requestedOn; inwardBespoke sets inwardOn; the piece is not in the PP queue until inwarded.

Production plan. created by submitPlan (forced over capacity is recorded) → closePlan sets closedOn. Extra fabric. requested → approved (metres deducted from the fabric) | declined → sent.

Return case (stage plus the last attempt's outcome).

  • raised (raiseReturn, slaDays 10) → startResolution(step, where): alteration at store (nothing travels) · alteration at factory or remake (MTM: factoryLeg opened) · alteration at hemming-unit (hemLeg opened, destination store) · refit · exchange (RTW: accepted and closed on the spot; stock moves) · store-credit (accepted, refunded, closed, ledger entry) · refund (refund requested, stage back-at-store, waits on finance).

  • factory leg: sendReturnToFactory (at-factory) → factoryReceivedReturn → returnDoneAtFactory (back-at-store) → store counts it in (storeInward('return') sets backAtStoreOn).

  • hemming leg: sendReturnToHemming (at-factory) → hemLegStep inwardAtWarehouse → atUnit → hemmed → backAtWarehouse → dispatched (back-at-store) → delivered.

  • decideResolution: accepted → closed; not enough → the next rung opens. moveRefund (finance; no button): refunded, closed. advanceReturn (raised → at-factory → back-at-store → refunded → closed) exists with no button.

Launch gates (launchOf). In production (no receivedAtHo) → At HO → Allocating (allocateLaunch) → Dispatching (dispatchLaunch) → Ready when all six gates hold (received at HO, allocated, all dispatched, all received, marketing brief, launch date) → Launched (updateLaunch({launchedOn})). Nothing in the model blocks launchedOn on the gates (blocking on name and dispatch is to build).

RTW option. active ⇄ inactive (decideOption('make-inactive' | 'liquidate') with reason; 'reintroduce' clears it and optionally adds incomingQty); 'promote-to-core' sets core; 'reorder' adds incomingQty (default 150). The verb is recomputed on every read, not nightly.

Swatch desk. Each of the seven dates is set once by its button, in any order (the screen offers the next unset one); dispatched also creates one SwatchDispatch per store, which the store confirms. Swatch books / fit samples. dispatched → received (store or storeInward); fit sample state ok / worn / missing by the store.

Vehicle round (receiveRound). per piece: received → RTS scan + one mtm-round GRN per factory; short → receiving.shortOn; rejected → back to in-factory with the reason appended to remarks.

Stock request. requested → accepted | declined | shipped → received (the asking store's storeInward('request') also sets received).

Sources: src/store.ts (every action listed and the fields it sets); src/domain/engines.ts (sourcingStateOf, conceptOf, launchOf, liveStatusOf, fabricPoStatus, sheetEditMode, returnStatusOf, ppQueue); src/screens/DesignHome.tsx (stage computation); src/domain/reference.ts (SHEET_FREE_EDIT, SHEET_STORE_EDIT, SHEET_AMEND, ORDER_STAGE_NEXT).

B5. Target Flow A — Requirement to PO, all four kinds

Design raises every new need from one list of design requirements, using one button, "+ New requirement". The first question is what kind of product it is: "An MTM fabric", "Several fabric options at once" or "An RTW style". Every requirement then moves through the same stages, and the list shows which one it is at: 1 Requirement, 2 Sourcing, 3 Finalised and ordered, 4 Downstream, then Live. The sourcing column always uses one of the same phrases: "sourcing to add vendors", "design to push PO", "extension asked" or "finalised". The several-options kind is covered in B6.

flowchart LR
  A[Design raises the need<br/>kind, name, why, wanted by] --> S[MTM only: production samples<br/>pattern file, PP round, shipment round]
  S --> P[Production posts the approval<br/>to planning, production, warehouse, stores]
  P --> B[Sourcing puts one vendor<br/>terms and hold-until date]
  A -->|RTW| B
  B --> C{Design answers}
  C -->|Push PO| D[PO raised, stage 3<br/>fabric added to the master, fabric PO number]
  C -->|Reject| B
  C -->|More time| E[Extension asked<br/>sourcing extends, sends back or drops the vendor]
  E --> B
#StepWhoWhat they doWhat they fill inWhat the system checksWhat happens nextTime target
B5.1Raise the requirementDesign (planning works on the same list)Click "+ New requirement" and pick the product type. For an MTM fabric, fill in the form and click "Log it". For an RTW style, fill in the form and click "Create requirement". Files such as the sketch, pattern file, CAD, swatch image or tech pack can be attached before savingMTM: kind ("Construction variant on an existing fabric" or "New fabric or shade"), working name and why. Under "more": swatch book or range (Formal Shirts Vol. 1 or Vol. 2, Casual Shirts Vol. 1, Linen Shirts, Leggiuno Bespoke) and the month it is wanted on the floor (Oct 26 to Feb 27). A construction variant also needs the fabric it is sampled on, plus collar, placket and cuff. RTW: category (seven garment categories), sub-category, style or colour, order quantity (300 by default), target in-house date (60 days out by default), target cost per piece (Rs 450 by default) and the size ratio across 28–50 or XS–4XL. BSC buys the cloth for every bulk style. The fabric brief covers cloth or weave, composition, colour, a close MTM fabric BSC already carries, and notes for sourcing. The size curve is spread automatically, weighted to the middle sizesMTM: name and why must be filled. The base fabric for a variant must be an inventory fabric with more than 20 m, not bespoke, and not paused or on hold. RTW: style and quantity must be filled. Minimum order and wanted-by date are not checkedThe requirement is recorded with who raised it and when. RTW requirements start with a default bill of materials and costing. The activity log records "Requirement created". An RTW requirement reaches sourcing only after design clicks "Send it to sourcing" — after the prototype is approved (B5.2R) and the BOM and tech pack are on it (B5.3R), carrying one fabric or a shortlist for sourcing to choose one from5 min
B5.2R(RTW) Prototype on a stand-in fabricDesign, with the production unitBefore any cloth is sourced, design asks the unit for a prototype of the style on whatever shirt fabric is to hand. The unit logs the proto when it comes back; design reviews it and clicks "Approve" or "Redo" with comments. Only an approved proto goes on to the fabric requirementStyle, the stand-in fabric used, the sketch or reference, and for each review comment the area and whether it is majorA new style cannot be sent to sourcing until a proto round is approved, or design marks "no proto needed" with a reason (a repeat, or a minor variant of a live style)The proto rounds (dates, reviewer, comments, decision) sit on the requirement. Design is reminded when a proto has waited on them 5 days7 to 10 days per round (to confirm with BSC)
B5.3R(RTW) Bill of materials and tech packDesignOnce the proto is approved, fill in the bill of materials, attach the tech pack, then click "Send it to sourcing"Fabric line(s): one fabric, or a shortlist of candidates for sourcing to choose one from, each with cloth or weave, composition, colour, a close MTM fabric BSC already carries, and notes. Trim lines: buttons, labels, thread, interlining, packaging, each with spec, quantity per piece and unit. The tech pack file"Send it to sourcing" is refused without a tech pack attached and at least one fabric line. The BOM is what the job card, the costing and the materials check are cut fromThe requirement moves to "sourcing to finalise the cloth". Sourcing sees it on Today with the shortlist; the trim lines are what B5.5R checks against stockSame day as the proto approval
B5.2(MTM · optional) Sample and pattern fileProduction unit, then designProduction picks the factory and clicks "Sample & PAT handed over". When the PP sample comes back, they click "Log PP sample · round N received from" the factory. Design reviews each round, adds comments with "+ add", then clicks "Approve", "Approve with comments" or "Send back — new round". The shipment sample (the harder gate) then goes through the same stepsFactory (the MTM factories are Mahendra, Richman and Harsh). For each review comment: the area (stitching, fit, pattern, construction, fabric handling, trims/labels/buttons, other), the comment, and a tick if it is majorThis step is optional. Design can click "Skip sampling" with a reason (a new shade of a fabric already in the book, a re-source of a known fabric, sourcing already holds an approved swatch) and go straight to the vendor and PO; the record keeps who skipped it and why. When sampling is run: the handover is refused unless a pattern file or CAD is attached, because the handoff is the file. "Approve" is blocked while a major comment is being drafted. "Send back" works only when there is a major commentThe factory, the date the pattern file went out, and each round (PP or shipment, dates, reviewer, comments, decision) are recorded. Design gets a reminder when a sample has been waiting on them for 5 days, and a warning after 107 to 14 days per round (BSC's own duration is to be confirmed with BSC)
B5.3(MTM · optional) Approval to every departmentProduction for now. The Invisit notes say design emails the approval to every department, stores included. Who owns this is to be confirmed with BSCWrite what to tell everyone and click "Post approval"The message. It defaults to "<name> cleared the shipment sample."Optional. Offered once the shipment round is approved, or once sampling was skipped with a reason; design can leave it out, and the fabric still moves on to the vendor and POThe approval date and sender are recorded. A post goes on the fabric's thread with the message and the construction, addressed to planning, production, warehouse and stores. It doubles as the training record for the fabric. Those teams see it on their Today list1 min
B5.4Sourcing puts one vendor against the askSourcingOpen the requirement, add the vendor and click "Save vendor", then "Send to design". For RTW this is done from the fabric sourcing list, which also suggests mills based on the fabrics they already supply. Where design sent a shortlist, sourcing finalises the cloth internally first (quotes, mills, minimums), picks the one fabric, records why, and adds the vendor for that fabricMTM, in three groups. Who: mill (the list shows how many days each mill holds cloth), article number and cloth name. Terms: price per metre, minimum order, lead time in days and hold-until date (defaults to today plus the mill's usual hold period). First buy (price × minimum order) is worked out. Cloth: composition, panna in inches, swatch book and a note for design. RTW: mill, composition, price per metre (e.g. Rs 320), minimum order (e.g. 200 m), lead time, width in cm (e.g. 57), hold-until date and a note. Fabric cost is worked outMTM needs mill, price and cloth name. RTW needs mill and price. Only one live vendor is allowed per ask: saving a second marks the first as "replaced by sourcing". Nothing stops sourcing adding a vendor before the sample is approved, even though sourcing is meant to go to the mills only after the shipment sample (whether to enforce this is to be confirmed with BSC)The vendor and terms are saved on the requirement, along with the date sent to design. RTW also records a committed delivery (today + lead time + 3 days) and moves to "design to push PO". Design gets a reminder: "N mill(s) quoted — choose the cloth" or "design to push PO". It becomes urgent when 5 days or less are left on the hold (3 for RTW) or the hold has lapsed. Sourcing gets a reminder if an RTW style has no vendor after 3 days3 to 10 days
B5.5Design answersDesignOpen the vendor card, type why, check the metres, then click "Push PO", "Reject" or "More time"Why (this stays on the record and goes back to sourcing with a rejection). Metres: MTM defaults to the minimum order. RTW suggests the larger of consumption × quantity or the minimum order, and warns if the figure is under the minimumA lapsed hold does not block "Push PO". The button is shown less prominently and the card says "hold lapsed — the price is not promised", but the click still goes through. "Reject" needs no reason. "More time" is hidden once an extension has already been askedPush PO (MTM): the vendor is marked chosen, with who, when and why. If the fabric is new, it is added to the fabric master. It gets a fabric code (NO + two letters of the mill + six letters of the name + a number), goes in the non-core bucket with a reorder threshold of 3 m (international) or 7 m (domestic), and starts on hold. A fabric PO number (FPO-71nn) is issued, due on today + lead time, with a remark naming the requirement, the chooser, the price and the article. Incoming metres go up, and any open extension is closed. Push PO (RTW): the vendor, metres and price are set on the costing, the style moves to "Allocated", and a fabric PO is issued. The metres are blocked, the fabric is added to the master and the milestone is recorded. Reject: the vendor is marked rejected with who, when and why. If no live vendor is left, the requirement goes back to "sourcing to add vendors". More time: the extension request is recorded and the requirement shows "extension asked". Who is told: after Push PO, the fabric appears on the swatch desk (MTM), on sourcing's list as "PO raised · get the cloth in", and on the warehouse's list of fabric rolls expected against POs. After Reject or More time, sourcing gets an urgent "extension asked" reminderSame day the terms arrive
B5.5R(RTW) Other BOM items into inventorySourcingAfter the fabric PO, go down the BOM's trim and packaging lines and check each against stock at the unit. Raise a bulk order where this order's need exceeds what is on the shelf, and click "Materials covered" when every line isPer line: on hand, needed for this order (quantity per piece × order quantity), and where short the bulk order and its expected dateAllocation can go ahead, but the materials step in B8 stays red until every BOM line is either on the shelf or on a dated order. A line still short seven days before cutting is an urgent item on sourcing's TodayEach line's cover is recorded against the order. The warehouse's list of fabric and trims expected gains the bulk ordersWithin 3 days of the fabric PO
B5.6Sourcing extends, sends back or dropsSourcingEnter a new hold date and click "Extend". Then click "Send back to design", or "Drop vendor" (MTM) or "Mill won't hold — withdraw vendor"New hold-until date (defaults to 14 days out)"Send back" works only when every live hold is in the futureThe hold date is updated, the extension is closed, and the requirement goes back to "design to push PO". Dropping a vendor marks it withdrawn ("the mill would not hold") and sends the requirement back to "sourcing to add vendors". Design is told1 to 3 days
B5.8Stage 3 and beyondSystemNothing to click. The stage is worked out from the record——MTM reaches stage 3 once the fabric PO is raised, stage 4 once the bulk is received or the fabric goes live, and Live when it is live in stores and online. RTW reaches stage 3 once the factory PO and the fabric PO are both raised. It reaches stage 4 once the cloth is at the factory or a sample is logged, and Live when the launch goes out. The downstream teams take over (B7, B8)—

Rules. Each ask has only one vendor at a time. A second vendor means the first was replaced, rejected or withdrawn, and the earlier one stays on the record with its reason. The design and sourcing status is always worked out from what has happened, never typed in. A lapsed hold shows red on the list and on the card, but only warns on "Push PO". Making it a hard stop is to be confirmed with BSC. Every edit to an RTW requirement goes into its activity log. MTM fabric asks have no activity log, so the decision lives on the vendor itself: who chose it, why, or why it was rejected. Design still creates the fabric datasheet in Enigma, because Enigma stays the master (22 Sep). The tool records who configured it in Enigma and when, and which designs are in Enigma. The fabric PO number the tool issues (FPO-71nn) is its own. The Enigma PO is still raised in Enigma until the link with Enigma is agreed (Section 15). An older route is still available on an MTM ask: design clicks "Choose" on a vendor, then records "Vendor has blocked it" (metres, how the mill confirmed it by email, WhatsApp or call, and an expiry of today + the mill's hold period, 60 days by default), then clicks "Raise the fabric PO" after approval. The block is marked converted when the PO is raised.

Cases. The record is the same in every case; only the steps shown change. The MTM path has six steps: Request, Sampling, Vendors, Purchase order, Fabric in, Go live. Steps that do not apply are hidden. There is no "sampling needed / not needed" question yet, and no link to the fabric being repeated. Later: add both, so a repeat or new shade can skip sampling with a reason and a parent fabric.

Time target. From raising to PO: 10 working days for a fabric the mill already knows, 25 for a new one. For MTM this runs from the date raised to the fabric PO date. For RTW it runs from the style handover to the fabric PO date. Later: show this figure on the list.

Sources: BSC design discovery call (22 Sep 2026); Invisit meeting notes §6.

B6. Target Flow B — Several options

When a swatch book is thin, design raises one request for several options. Each option (1 to 20) is settled on its own. Every option that is settled becomes its own MTM fabric and its own row on the swatch desk. As the team puts it, "seven of ten going live is a normal outcome, not a failure."

flowchart LR
  A[Book short of the plan of 30<br/>link fills in the request] --> B[Request: category,<br/>count, brief, a word per option, bill of materials]
  B --> C[Sourcing fills an option<br/>one vendor, terms, hold-until, send to design]
  C --> D{Design, per option}
  D -->|Push PO| E[Option PO raised<br/>new fabric, fabric PO, swatch desk row]
  D -->|Reject| C
  D -->|More time| F[Extension] --> C
  D -->|Close with a reason| G[Closed, not counted]
  E --> H[Every open option has a PO<br/>request at stage 3]
#StepWhoWhat they doWhat they fill inWhat the system checksWhat happens nextTime target
B6.1The gap is seenDesignLook at the "Active options per book · plan 30" strip on the fabric asks list, or the swatch book tiles on the fabric master. Click "raise a request for N options" to start a request with the details already filled inNothingPer book, the system counts active fabrics: those in the book that are not bespoke, on hold or paused. It also counts "coming" fabrics, which have a PO in that book. It shows how many the book is short of the plan of 30. The plan is one number for every book. Setting a plan per range is to be confirmed with BSCThe request opens with the count, category "MTM shirts" and a brief ("Assortment gap — <book> N short; …") already filled inAlways visible
B6.2Raise the requestDesign (or planning)Click "+ New requirement", then "Several fabric options at once". Fill in the form and click "Raise the request → sourcing"Category (the seven garment categories plus MTM shirts, default RTW CMT shirts). How many options (1 to 20, default 8). Live by (Oct 26 to Feb 27). Target price per piece (default Rs 600). Why ("the sentence from the meeting"). A word per option, such as "a brushed check" or "plain twill, dark". Bill of materials, one line per item: item, spec, quantity per pieceWhy must be filled. The count is kept between 1 and 20A request number (REQ-nnn) is issued, with who raised it and when. Each option starts as open, and the bill of materials starts with shell fabric at 1.6 m plus trims. Sourcing gets a reminder after 3 days: "REQ-nnn · <category> — sourcing to add vendors on N options"5 min
B6.3Sourcing fills an optionSourcingOpen the request and go to the options. Pick an option marked "your move" and click "Put a vendor against option N". Click "Save vendor", then "Send option N to design". Click "Edit" to change it laterThe same vendor form as B5.4: mill, article, cloth name, price per metre, minimum order, lead time, hold-until date, composition, panna, swatch book and a note. First buy is worked outMill, price and name must be filled. Only one live vendor is allowed per option, so a second one replaces the first ("replaced by sourcing"). A warning shows when price per metre × fabric per piece is more than 60% of the target cost. The trims in the bill of materials show stock from the trims master ("covers ~N pcs", "below threshold")The option moves to design, with the date it was sent. Design gets a reminder: "REQ-nnn · option N · <note> — design to push PO". It becomes urgent when 5 days or less are left on the holdPer option
B6.4Design settles the optionDesign or planningOpen the option (a link from the reminder lands straight on it), type why, check the metres, then click "Push PO · Rs <metres × price>", "Reject" or "More time"Why. Metres (defaults to the minimum order)Same as B5.5: a lapsed hold only warns, and "Reject" needs no reason. "Push PO" is refused only if the option is closed or already has a POPush PO: the vendor is marked chosen, with who, when and why. The fabric is added to the master (as in B5.5) and a fabric PO number (FPO-71nn) is issued, with a remark naming the request, option, category, chooser, price and article. The option shows "PO raised". An MTM fabric record is created for the option, already approved, due on the live-by month, with no designs yet. The option and its swatch desk row are one record. Reject: the vendor is marked rejected. If every vendor on the option is rejected, it goes back to open. More time: the option shows "extension asked" and the request is recorded. Who is told: sourcing's list, the swatch desk and the warehouse's list of fabric POsSame day the offer arrives
B6.5Close an option without a PODesign or sourcingClick "Not going ahead with option N?", give a reason and click "Close option N". Design can reopen itReasonAn option that already has a PO cannot be closedThe option is marked closed, with who, when and why. Any open extension is closed. The request row shows "N closed"—
B6.6The request completesSystemNothing to click—Every option that is not closed has a POThe request moves to stage 3, "Finalised and ordered". Each option's fabric then runs through B7 on its own—

Rules. An option has one vendor at a time. Earlier vendors stay underneath as rejected or withdrawn history. The request row shows one dot per option in the colour of the team holding it: green once the PO is raised, grey when closed. It also shows the counts in the same shared phrases ("2 PO raised · 2 design to push PO · 4 sourcing to add vendors · 1 extension asked"). Closed options do not count against the request. When design asks for more time, sourcing clicks "Extend" for each vendor, then "Hold extended — back to design". That button is blocked while any live hold has lapsed. Sourcing can also click "Mill won't hold — withdraw vendor", which sends the option back to open. Planning sees the same screens as design.

Cases.

CaseWhat differs
Fill a book ("ten winter shirts")Set the category, count and live-by date, and write a word per option. Sourcing fills the options in any order and picks the swatch book on the vendor form
Replace named fabrics ("the blue madras is going, find two like it")The option note can name the fabric. Nothing links the option to the old fabric, and the old fabric is not paused automatically. Later: link the old fabric and pause it when the new one goes live
Design rejects an option twiceEach rejection sends the option back to open. There is no "close after two" prompt. Closing is always done with "Not going ahead…"
Sourcing cannot find oneSourcing closes the option with a reason ("no mill has this weight under the target"). The request completes with fewer fabrics and the row says "N closed"
Design wants one option fastOptions cannot be tagged urgent. Design's reminders are ordered by how many hold days are left, fewest first
Holds lapse while design decidesEach option has its own extension. The request row counts "N extension asked". Design's reminder becomes urgent at 5 days or less, and after that says "the hold lapsed N days ago"
Trims for the requestThe trims in the bill of materials show stock, threshold and incoming from the trims master. A trim not on the master is flagged "add it there"

Time target. From raising the request to settling every option: 15 working days. This runs from the date raised to the date of the last option's fabric PO. Later: show this figure on the request.

Sources: C1a MTM call (27 Aug 2026) for the range-mix rule; BSC swatch book list.

B7. Target Flow C — MTM downstream: swatch desk, designs, sticker, go live

Raising the fabric PO puts the fabric on the swatch desk, which replaces the MTM swatches tracker sheet. As the cloth moves, the warehouse records six dates: bulk received, to swatches, cut, sticker, dispatched and photos. Sourcing adds the designs. Design tells the stores and switches the fabric on whenever it decides, because go live is a button, not a gate. Online is a separate track, about two weeks behind.

Two warehouse people on the desk. Each step on the swatch desk is marked by the person who did it, and the record keeps who it was. Bulk received, handover and dispatch are Shravani's. Cut, sticker and photos are Yusuf's: the design codes and the sticker file reach him on the record the moment design adds the designs, so he never waits for a file to be made by hand. Stores told is design's.

flowchart LR
  A[PO raised] --> B[Bulk received<br/>warehouse marks the date]
  B --> C[To swatches] --> D[Cut 25 x 25] --> E[Sticker printed<br/>from the record, one label per design]
  E --> F[Dispatched<br/>one parcel per store, 34 stores] --> G[Photos]
  G --> H[Stores told<br/>design]
  A -.-> I[Designs on the fabric<br/>sourcing, in parallel]
  I --> J[Set up in Enigma<br/>flags, name and price]
  H --> K[Go live in stores<br/>any time]
  K --> L[Online about 2 weeks later<br/>photos, video, post, live]
  A -.-> M[Fabric rolls received<br/>warehouse, separate list]

B7.12Go live in storesDesignWrite the story, pick the channels and click "Save the story". Then click "Go live in stores", or "Go live" on the desk rowWhat the store and the website say. Channels: stores, website, swatch books, socialNothing is blocked ("never gated; your call, the record keeps what was skipped"). The button shows how many gates are still openThe live date is recorded, the fabric comes off hold, and the story is saved. Stores and marketing see it, and the desk row moves to "Live in stores"Design's call
B7.13OnlineDesignUnder "Live online", click "Mark done" for each of swatch photography, video, post-production and product page ready. Then click "Go live online"NothingOnly after B7.12. "Go live online" works only once photography, video and post-production are all doneThe online dates are recorded. The requirement reaches Live only when it is live in stores and onlineAbout 14 days after B7.12

Rules. The desk has a row for every new fabric with a PO or swatch work started. It can be filtered: all, to cut and sticker, to dispatch, to tell stores, live in stores. Priority rows come first, then the rows that have waited longest since the bulk arrived. The warehouse marks its six dates in order. Design owns "Stores told" and "Go live". Stickers are only made in the tool: the empty sticker link column on the old tracker becomes the sticker date. A fabric shows on the fabric master's swatch books from the PO onwards, marked active, coming, on hold or paused, and "sells as" its designs. The store's order form lists the fabric first, then the design on it. "The fabric only, no design chosen" is allowed. The design's product code goes on the order. Enigma stays the master for the fabric's details. The tool only records that they were set up, by whom and when.

Cases.

CaseWhat differs
A fabric with no designs yetIt sells as the fabric. The sticker carries the fabric lines only, and the desk row says "no designs yet" in amber
A second lot of a fabric already in the booksOnly the fabric GRN is booked in the warehouse. No new swatch desk row is created. A new edition would need a new ask
Priority-1Sorts to the top. The warehouse reminder fires as soon as it is marked priority and not cut, with no waiting days. A normal row reminds after 2 days in without handover
Store-only fabric (never online)There is no separate option. The online track simply stays unmarked after stores. Whether to mark a fabric store-only is to be confirmed with BSC
International clothSame steps. The PO's due date is the offer's lead time. The warehouse's list of fabric POs shows the mill's date as "not confirmed" until sourcing records the mill's confirmation
A store never confirmsThe store's own list turns "overdue" after 7 days. The desk keeps showing "N of 34 received". No second parcel is sent automatically
Fabric paused before stores are toldDesign pauses it with a note, which hides it from stores and the order form. The desk row is not closed automatically. Later: close the desk row when the fabric is paused
Cloth from a several-options requestArrives on the desk already approved by the request, with the brief "REQ-nnn · option N …"

Time target. From arrival to stores told: 12 working days domestic, 30 international. This runs from the bulk received date to the stores told date. The desk shows both dates, but not yet the gap. Later: show the gap on the desk.

Sources: BSC design discovery call (22 Sep 2026); Granola notes "BSC - Design/Planning" (22 Sep 2026); MTM swatches tracker sheet; BSC store list.

B8. Target Flow D — RTW style, request to launch (the four-section record)

An RTW style runs on one record with four sections, the same four the production team's sheet has. Design writes section 1, the request. Nitin, who runs RTW production, runs section 2, factory finalisation, and agrees it with design. Sourcing runs section 3, fabric finalisation, at the same time. Nitin and the factory merchandiser keep section 4, the time-and-action milestones and final steps, up to date. Each section shows the sheet's own columns and nothing else. Files and the conversation sit at the foot of the record.

The record is on the RTW production tracker from the day design sends the request. Until design raises the factory purchase order it reads "no PO yet", and those rows sort first. Planning no longer allocates a factory on a capacity board: Nitin agrees a factory on the phone and proposes it. BSC buys the cloth for every bulk style.

flowchart TD
  A[Design · request: style, quantity, size ratio, target price and in-house date, proto on a stand-in fabric] --> B[Production · Nitin proposes a factory, cost and in-house date]
  A --> F[Sourcing · one vendor against the cloth: price, MOQ, lead time, mill's article, hold]
  B --> C{Design accepts?}
  C -->|no · with a comment| B
  C -->|yes| D[Design raises the factory PO · job card, trim card, BOM and tech pack travel with it]
  F --> G{Design pushes the fabric PO?}
  G -->|more time · sourcing extends the hold| F
  G -->|yes| H[Enigma fabric code minted · the tracker's fabric details fill in]
  D --> I[Sourcing sends the cloth to the factory]
  H --> I
  I --> J[Factory merchandiser counts usable and damaged cloth, confirms trims]
  J -->|short| K[Modified ratio or cut short · design decides]
  J -->|in full| L[Milestones · samples · QC · dispatch, B12b]
  K --> L
  L --> M[Warehouse receives the lots · go live · stock sold to customers by the warehouse]
#StepWhoWhat they doWhat they fill inWhat the system checksWhat happens nextTime target
B8.1Request from designDesignStarts a new RTW style, has a proto made on a stand-in fabric (B5.2R, optional for a repeat), and sends the request.Style, category, fit, order quantity, size ratio across 28–50 or XS–4XL, target price, target in-house date, the cloth wanted, trims, and the job card, trim card, BOM and tech packThe size ratio adds up to the order quantity. Documents not attached yet are listed as owed; they do not block the request.Day 0

B8.3Factory decisionDesignAccepts the proposal or rejects it with a comment.Accept or reject, commentA rejection needs a comment.Rejected: back to Nitin with the comment, and he proposes again. Every proposal and decision stays on the record. Accepted: design can raise the factory PO.+1 day
B8.4Factory PODesignRaises the factory PO. The job card, trim card, BOM and tech pack travel with it.—The proposal must be accepted.The PO number and PO date are set. The milestones are drawn from the category's template, counted from the PO date (B12b.1). Owed documents are flagged on the PO. The factory merchandiser sees the PO on their portal.Same day
B8.5A vendor against the clothSourcingRuns the same loop as MTM (B6): from the day the request lands, puts one vendor against the cloth.Mill, mill's article, price per metre, MOQ, lead time, hold-until dateOne live vendor per cloth.Design is told the vendor is ready.+3 days
B8.6Fabric PODesignPushes the fabric PO, asks for more time, or rejects the vendor.Metres, reasonThe hold has not lapsed; if it has, sourcing extends it first.The fabric PO is raised and the Enigma fabric code is minted. The tracker's fabric details fill in by themselves: vendor, vendor fabric code, Enigma code, cost per metre and metres blocked.Same day
B8.7Trims and packagingSourcingGets the other BOM items into stock (B5.5R).Received quantities—Trims are marked ready on the PO when every trim line is in.Before cutting
B8.8Cloth to the factorySourcingSends the cloth once both POs exist.Date, metres sentThe factory PO must exist.The factory merchandiser sees the cloth on its way.Within the mill's lead time
B8.9Receipt at the factoryFactory merchandiserConfirms on their portal what arrived.Metres usable, metres damaged, trims received, note—In full: the fabric milestone's actual date is set. Short: B8.10.On arrival
B8.10Short clothFactory merchandiser or production, then designAsks for a modified ratio or a cut short.Reason, proposed size breakup—Design gives the new ratio. It is sent to the factory and the order quantity updates.+1 day
B8.11Milestones and final stepsNitin, factory merchandiserSamples, milestones, QC, dispatch, as B12b.———Per template
B8.12Receipt, design code, go liveWarehouse, design, planningThe warehouse receives each lot (B12.3). Once the garments are in, design mints the design code (the product code the style sells under). Planning puts the style into a drop.Counts—The design code is a gate on the launch: nothing can be made live without it. The style goes live. RTW customer orders are stock sales that the warehouse fills from stock.Same day

Rules. Section 2 and section 3 run side by side; neither waits for the other until the cloth is sent. The cloth can be sent only when both POs exist. The fabric details on the tracker are never typed by hand; they come from the fabric PO. Design owns the request, the factory decision and both POs. Nitin owns the proposal. Sourcing owns the vendor and the cloth. The factory merchandiser owns the receipt and the milestones.

Cases.

CaseWhat changes
Repeat styleThe proto is skipped with a reason; the earlier factory can be proposed again.
Design late with the tech packThe PO goes out with the tech pack flagged as owed; the factory merchandiser sees it missing.
Factory rejected twiceEach proposal and comment stays on the record; Nitin proposes a third.
Mill's hold lapsesDesign asks for more time; sourcing extends the hold or withdraws the vendor and puts another.
Cloth short at the factoryThe merchandiser asks for a modified ratio or a cut short; design decides.

Time targets. Request to factory PO within 3 days. Request to fabric PO within 4 days. Cloth at the factory before the template's fabric date.

Sources: call with Nitin, RTW production, 3 Oct 2026; Production Tracker Final V2.xlsx (Production Tracker and Instructions & Legend tabs); design discovery session, 22 Sep 2026.

B10. Target Flow F — Store: bills, cases, alterations, store credit

The store sells one piece at a time and resolves problems one piece at a time. A customer's visit is one bill that can hold several pieces. A complaint is a case on one piece that has already been delivered. The store manager's first question is always "can I fix it here?". The store's page has five groups:

Added from the store manager call, 13 Oct 2026. The bill carries, per piece, the fit profile used (a customer can hold several: "Formal · structured", "Casual · relaxed"), who measured, a comment for the factory and a comment for shipping; both comments travel on the order sheet so nobody retypes them. A new customer is marked "trial shirt first" and the promise becomes about 25 days; "urgent" makes it 7–8 days with the ₹600 express charge on the bill. The store's bell tells it when a fabric has been closed for selling in the last fortnight, so the swatch comes off the book. A credit note is good for a year; a full refund to the bank waits for HO approval before finance moves it; a case opened within 30 days of delivery reads "free alteration, credit note on return", after that it reads chargeable. A fabric cannot go live in stores until its composition and a description are on the record, and a name or price change after the sticker was printed flags the sticker for reprint on the swatch desk. Production can move an open piece's ship-by date with a reason, one piece from its sheet or every open piece at a factory that is shut, and the store sees the old date, the new one and why, with a bell item to tell the customer. One bill carries pieces of different sizes, inseams and fits. Every store sees every store's shelf for a style: who sells it off the rack and how many per size, who only shows it, and whose window is empty, with an ask to the store that has it from the cell. Every store gets one display piece per launched style; it is not shelf stock, but the store may sell it, which puts its window at minus one until the next order brings one, and the owed display pieces sit at the top of the shelf view with an order button. When a piece is taken back, a numbered credit note is generated on the customer's account the moment the case is raised, good for a year; a refund to the bank stays the exception that needs approval. Still to build: stickers and barcodes printed from the system, the daily closing report read off the bills, and measurements that update every open order when a profile changes.

  • customers

  • orders (made to measure and ready to wear)

  • what I can sell: the fabrics live today, plus swatches, books and fit samples

  • stock in and out: parcels received from the unit, stock asks, launches, pull-backs and damaged pieces

  • returns and alterations

Anyone who is not from that store, and is not the owner, can see the page but cannot act on it. A strip tells them the store acts on it.

B10-i. Punch a bill

#StepWhoWhat they doWhat they fill inWhat the system checksWhat happens nextTime target
B10.1Customer, then piecesStore associateOpens "Punch MTM / RTW order". Enters the customer, then adds pieces one by one with "+ Piece". Each piece lands in "On the bill so far", where it can be edited or removed. Ends with "Punch bill". If the customer holds store credit, a note reads "holds ₹x store credit" and offers "Apply to this bill" or "use all of it".Customer name as it appears on the bill. For each piece, choose made to measure or off the shelf.<br><br>Made to measure: fabric, design on this fabric (optional; shows the product code and MRP), fit (Tailored, Classic, Slim or the customer's saved fit), quantity, and the order sheet (22 construction options, 20 measurements, 4 pattern flags). The sheet is pre-filled from the customer's last order when the bill is started from the customer's profile.<br><br>Off the shelf: style, size (shows how many are here and which other store has it), and inseam in inches for bottoms, otherwise quantity.<br><br>Promised date and remarks sit behind "more".The customer name is filled in. Each made-to-measure piece has a fabric. Only live fabrics can be picked: not paused, not on hold, available at the mill, and with saleable metres or bespoke. A hint shows how many metres are saleable across all stores. Each off-the-shelf piece has a style and a size, and only styles with stock can be picked. If the size is not in this store, a warning names the store that has it, or says the hub will send it. Credit applied can never be more than the customer holds.One bill number is issued (BILL-xxxxx), plus one order number per piece (BSC-731xx).<br><br>Promised dates, unless the associate sets one: 14 days for made to measure, today for off the shelf, 3 days for bottoms that need hemming.<br><br>Made to measure: the fabric needed (Enigma's base metres plus 0.10 m, or 1.85 m for linen) is held back at once for every store. A bespoke fabric gets its bespoke code.<br><br>Off the shelf: the size leaves the shelf count.<br><br>Credit used is written against the customer.<br><br>Planning sees made-to-measure pieces in its plan queue. The hub sees off-the-shelf pieces this store does not have. The customer's profile shows the bill.During the visit

The store's order list shows, for each piece:

  • order and bill number (with "n pcs" when the bill has more than one piece)

  • customer, fabric or style, fit or size, and inseam for off-the-shelf bottoms

  • quantity and the day punched

  • the promised date, shown in red with the days late

  • the stage

Each order has one button. "Hand over from shelf" appears when the size is on this store's shelf. "Handed to customer" appears once the hub has sent it.

Stages for made to measure: punched, awaiting plan, planned, in production, in the factory, out of the factory, ready to ship, dispatched, delivered. For off the shelf: punched, hemming, dispatched, delivered — a stock sale handled by the warehouse, never a factory.

Opening an order shows:

  • the bill's other pieces

  • the history of the order sheet and a panel for changing it

  • files and a conversation

The store can change the order sheet in three stages:

  • Freely, until production allocates a factory.

  • Remarks only, after allocation. The store sees: "Production has allocated a factory, so the sheet is closed to the store."

  • No changes, once the piece is made. The store sees: "A change now is an alteration or a remake."

Every accepted change is recorded with what changed, from what, to what, who changed it and when.

B10-ii. A customer comes back

flowchart TD
  A[Case on the piece<br/>order or bill no, or name] --> B{Can we fix it here?}
  B -->|yes| C[Alter in store<br/>own tailor or hemming]
  B -->|another size on the shelf| D[Swap from the shelf<br/>case closes, stock moves]
  B -->|no| E{Send it out}
  E --> F[Hemming unit<br/>through the warehouse, 7 hand-offs]
  E --> G[Back to the factory<br/>made to measure: alter or remake]
  C --> H{Customer happy?}
  F --> H
  G --> H
  H -->|yes| I[Closed]
  H -->|no| J[Refit or remake]
  J --> H
  H -->|still no| K[Store credit closes it<br/>or refund goes to finance]
#StepWhoWhat they doWhat they fill inWhat the system checksWhat happens nextTime target
B10.2Raise the caseStore managerPresses "Return or alter" on the delivered piece, or "+ Raise a return" in returns. Types the customer's name, the order number (for example BSC-74078) or the bill number into one search box. A name lists every piece delivered to that customer ("n delivered on m bills"). A number narrows it to one piece. The chosen piece shows its type, bill, factory and which version of the order sheet it was made to, and whether the customer holds store credit.Reason: fit, fabric defect, stitching, wrong size sent, changed mind, or colour or shade. What the customer wants: refund, remake or exchange. Remarks.Only pieces delivered from this store can be found.A case number is issued (RET-41xx) with a 10-day promise. The case shows on the order ("Cases on this piece"), in the store's returns list and on the customer's profile.During the visit
B10.3Decide, right nowStore managerOpens "What to do · the ladder". The six rungs show as a row of dots: alteration, swap from the shelf, refit, remake, store credit, refund. Below them is a card for each option not yet tried:<br><br>Fix it here: "Alter it in the store".<br>Swap from the shelf (off the shelf only): one button per other size on this shelf with its count, or "No other size… ask another store".<br>Send it out for the work: "To the hemming unit" and, for made to measure, "Back to the factory".<br>Refit (made to measure): "Re-measure".<br>Remake (made to measure; highlighted once an alteration has failed): "Remake at the factory · ~14 days".<br>Give the money back: "Store credit · close" and "Refund".<br><br>The screen says "Skipping a rung is your call and stays on the record."One line on what was agreed with the customer. For a swap, the size. For an alteration, where it happens: in the store, at the factory or at the hemming unit.Only untried options are offered.Each choice is recorded as an attempt with its date, who made it and the note.<br><br>Swap: the case closes at once and the new size leaves the shelf count. The returned piece goes back on the shelf only when the reason was fit, changed mind or wrong size sent.<br>Store credit: the case closes. The customer is credited the fabric's or style's MRP, and the credit shows on their profile.<br>Refund: goes to finance's list for today, and the case waits.<br>Factory alteration or remake: a trip to the factory opens, and production is told.<br>Hemming-unit alteration: a trip to the unit opens, and it appears on the warehouse's alterations list.First decision in the same visit
B10.4Hemming-unit round tripStore, then warehouse, then production unit, then warehouse, then storeFollows "The piece's round trip · store → warehouse → hemming unit → warehouse → store". There are seven hand-offs, each with a dot and the team that owns it:<br>1. handed to the vehicle by the store<br>2. received at the warehouse<br>3. scanned in at the hemming unit<br>4. hemmed and scanned out<br>5. back at the warehouse<br>6. sent to the store or straight to the customer<br>7. received at the store, or delivered and confirmed by the store<br><br>Whoever holds the piece presses the one button for their hand-off. Everyone else sees which team acts next.When handing it over, the store picks where it comes back to: the store (the customer collects) or straight to the customer.Each hand-off can be pressed only after the one before it.Each hand-off is dated. The case shows a plain status such as "at the hemming unit" or "on its way to the customer", and which team it is waiting on. The next team is told at each hand-off. The warehouse and the unit see every piece in flight with the days since it left the store.5 working days, store to store
B10.5Factory round trip (made to measure)Store, then productionFollows "The piece's round trip · store → factory → store". The factory that made the piece is already chosen. The piece goes through four hand-offs:<br>1. handed to the vehicle by the store<br>2. scanned in at the factory (production)<br>3. altered or remade, and on its way back (production)<br>4. back at the store and counted in (store, from the list of parcels received from the unit)Which factory, if it is not the one that made it.Each hand-off only after the one before it.Each hand-off is dated on the case. Production is told first. Then the piece appears on the store's list of parcels coming in.12 working days, store to store
B10.6CloseStore managerAnswers "Did the (step) work?" with "Close · happy" or "Not enough".A note.Offered only when the last attempt is still open, no refund is waiting, and the piece is not out on a trip."Close · happy" closes the case. "Not enough" brings back the cards for the options not yet tried. The next suggested option is the first untried one in this order: alteration, refit, remake, store credit, refund. A swap is a side door, not a rung.Inside the 10-day promise

B10-iii. Store credit. Each customer has a credit ledger. A case that gives credit adds to it, and a bill that spends credit takes from it. Each entry records the store, the case or order, who made it and when. The balance shows in several places:

  • as a "Store credit" column on the customer list

  • on the customer's profile, with the last three entries

  • as a note on the bill form and on the raise-a-case form

  • on the "Give the money back" card ("they already hold ₹x")

The system never lets a bill spend more than the balance. It applies whichever is smaller, the amount asked or the balance, and records nothing if that is zero. The amount credited is the fabric's or style's MRP, because the bill value lives in Shopify.

B10-iv. Stock in and out.

Received from the unit. One row for each parcel on its way. A parcel can be:

Fewer pieces than the box said. On a stock transfer the store can count in fewer than were sent: it enters how many arrived and what was missing, and counts those in. The STO closes at the counted figure, the goods-received note says "short, 3 of 5", and the warehouse gets the gap on its own list and in its alerts. The warehouse closes it one of two ways: send the balance, which appears at the store under Received from the unit like any parcel, or write it off with a reason that stays on the STO. The store never has to call to say a size was missing.

Asking for swatch books. From Swatches, books & fit samples the store asks the unit for books: which book, how many copies, and why (a second stylist, a worn book, a corporate visit). The ask sits on the warehouse's book list with the reason. The warehouse dispatches the copies, which then run like any book dispatch and the store confirms when they land, or declines with a note the store sees. The store can see every ask it has made and where each one is.

  • a stock transfer, marked "arrived, not counted in" or "in transit", with its transfer type and hemming type

  • a customer's made-to-measure shirt, or a customer's off-the-shelf order

  • launch display and fit pieces

  • a swatch book or a loose swatch

  • stock from another store

  • a made-to-measure fit sample

  • a returned piece back from the factory

Each row shows the date sent (in red after 5 days), what it is, where it came from and the quantity. It has one button: "Counted in · close STO", "Received · ready for customer" or "Received". Counting in does four things:

  • closes the transfer with its arrival date

  • marks a customer order as delivered

  • marks a launch piece, book, swatch, fit sample or returned piece as received

  • writes a numbered store goods-received note that the unit sees

Damaged and unsellable. The manager fills in:

  • style, and size (showing how many the system holds)

  • quantity

  • why: damaged, soiled, display worn, lost, or wrong item received

  • what happens to it: sent back to the unit, or destroyed

Then they press "Write off · stock drops now". The shelf count drops at once. The write-off stays open so the unit can stamp the day it gets the piece back.

Stock asks. "Someone has it" lists only the sizes this store is out of and another store holds. The ask goes to one named store ("Ask Pali Hill for 1"). The warehouse row says it "replenishes on the STO cycle". The other store answers "Accept & ship" or "Decline", and then "Shipped". The asking store presses "Received". The statuses run requested, accepted, shipped, then received or declined.

Launches. Display and fit pieces per launch, with "Received — displays up". These pieces are to show, not to sell. They are issued at cost and are not in the shelf count.

Swatches, books and fit samples. The made-to-measure fit set is a grid of 3 fits (slim, standard, heavy) by sizes 38, 40, 42 and 44. Each cell reads on the floor, worn out, gone, never arrived, or on its way. The store marks "worn", "gone" or "it's fine". The production unit sees the gaps and sends replacements. The sizes are a placeholder: the store floor mentioned 36 to 50, so the set stores carry is to be confirmed with BSC. Swatch books are confirmed with "Received — on the floor", and loose swatches with "Confirm it arrived".

Pull back. Every style that planning has marked liquidate or exit, which "comes off the display and goes back to the warehouse".

Made-to-measure fabrics live today. Saleable metres and a status:

  • active

  • low stock, below 15 m, with "roughly n shirts left across all stores" at 2.15 m a shirt

  • sold out

  • on hold

  • paused

Each status comes with a line on what to tell the customer.

Rules.

  • A case is always on a piece delivered from this store. A piece sold elsewhere is raised at that store.

  • The ladder is recorded in order. Skipping a rung is allowed, and the skip is recorded.

  • A swap closes on the spot. It moves stock both ways only when the piece itself is fine.

  • Store credit is the store's decision and closes the case. Only a refund waits on finance, and the store sees the moment finance moves it.

  • An alteration the store does itself never leaves the store's page.

  • A piece out on a trip cannot be closed until it is back.

  • The store changes an order sheet only until a factory is allocated.

  • A write-off drops stock the same minute.

  • A stock ask goes to one named store, never to "someone".

  • An off-the-shelf order is a stock sale, like the website: it can be punched only for a size in stock at this store or the unit, it never goes to a factory, and what this store does not hold the warehouse sends (B12.12).

Cases.

CaseWhat differs on the case
Made to measure · fitOrder of options: fix it here (store tailor), then the hemming unit or factory, then refit, then remake, then money. No swap card; swapping is only for off-the-shelf pieces.
Made to measure · fabric defect or stitching"Fix it here" is still offered, but the factory route is the natural one. Remake becomes the main option once an alteration has failed.
Off the shelf · wrong size sent or fitSwap from the shelf first. The returned piece goes back on the shelf, because the reason is fit, changed mind or wrong size sent. Alteration comes second.
Off the shelf · bottoms lengthFix it here if the store has a tailor, otherwise the hemming unit. The inseam on the order is the reference. Later: record the corrected inseam on the order.
Changed mind, unwornOn a swap the piece goes back to stock. Otherwise it is store credit or refund. Later: hide the alteration cards for this reason.
Colour or shadeA swap or money. Later: hide the alteration cards for this reason; today the cards do not look at the reason.
Customer is not in the store (online order)The hemming-unit trip can be sent straight to the customer. Later: the online team raises cases on online orders from their own page.
Second case on the same pieceShown as "Cases on this piece" on the order. Later: refuse a second open case on the same piece.
Store has no tailor"Fix it here" is always shown today. Later: a per-store setting to hide it.
Refund above a set limitNo limit is set; the limit is to be confirmed with BSC. Finance sees every refund on its list for today, with the amount and the days waiting.
Case past its promiseEvery case carries a 10-day promise. When it is overdue, the store is alerted: "RET-x · n d open against a 10 d promise".

Time targets.

  • Case raised to first decision: in the same visit.

  • Hemming-unit round trip: 5 working days, from the day the store hands it to the vehicle to the day it is delivered.

  • Factory round trip: 12 working days, from the day it is sent to the day it is back at the store.

  • Refund moved by finance: 2 days. Finance's alert turns amber after 2.

  • Parcel counted in: the day the box is opened. It turns red on the store's list after 5 days.

  • Stock ask answered: the same day. It stays on the other store's list for today until answered.

Sources: store visit transcript (section 7, the ladder); Enigma Answers (26 Aug 2026) for the Enigma sheet fields; Discovery Record — What We Know v1.0 (18 Aug 2026).

B11. Target Flow G — Replenishment and stock health

One rule for MTM core fabrics and one action word per RTW option, both worked out afresh every time someone opens the list (there is no overnight run yet; in the live system a scheduled recalculation does the same job) and shown as to-do queues with buttons, never as a sheet to scan.

B11-i. MTM core — in the MTM fabric master, "What to buy" → "Core · reorder points". The rule is Enigma's own, as BSC described it, and each fabric's page shows it factor by factor under "Auto-PO threshold · Enigma's rule":

sellable stock    = physical stock less metres reserved for orders, less the fabric's floor
                    (the 3 m / 7 m floor keeps cloth back from sale; it is not the reorder point)
rate of sale      = the average shirts sold per week over the last 8 weeks
metres a week     = rate of sale × 2.15 m per shirt
lead-time demand  = metres a week × the mill's lead time in days ÷ 7, rounded up
reorder point     = the larger of lead-time demand and the mill's minimum order
                    (200 m when the mill has no minimum)
below the point   = sellable stock plus metres on order is at or under the reorder point
                    (never applies to bespoke fabrics)
auto-PO quantity  = the gap between the reorder point and (sellable + on order),
                    rounded up to whole minimum orders, and never less than one minimum order
#StepWhoWhat they doWhat they fill inWhat the system checksWhat happens nextTime target
B11.1Every core fabric is read against its reorder pointSystem, each time the list is openedShows "Core · reorder points" with filters: All · Raise the PO by hand · International · Under the point · Sold out, still sellable · Paused by design · Switched off · Mill check due. Each row says what actually happens to its reorder: "Enigma auto-PO handles this", "no automation — international, a person raises it", or "covered by the PO".— (reads fabric stock, reserved metres, floor, weekly sales, metres on order, the mill's lead time and minimum order from the vendor master, and whether the fabric is international)Later: weeks when the fabric was out of stock will be left out of the 8-week average, as the RTW side already doesNothing is stored; the numbers are worked out each time. Sourcing sees how many fabrics are under the point, and "Mill check due" once 7 days have passed since the mill was last asked.Live

The fabric's live status follows: Paused → On hold → Sold out (nothing sellable) → Low stock (under 15 m) → Active. Stores see a paused or on-hold fabric marked "cannot be ordered — offer the closest alternative" on their MTM fabrics list.Same day

B11-ii-a. RTW planning review (8 Oct 2026). Planning only plans RTW, and does it in sheets fed from Shopify exports. The first build is one record per option that holds what those sheets hold, and a review the planner can inspect and override. Nothing in it is a forecast model; that comes later, once the record has trustworthy sales, availability, delivery and decision history to test against.

#StepWhoWhat they doWhat the system shows or checksWhat happens next
B11.P1Open the optionPlanningPicks the option from the RTW planning list, worst cover at arrival first.Type: core (same option, a different quantity), non-core (replace or let it run out) or new (under eight weeks old). Usable stock by size; display and trial pieces beside it, never inside it; what is on the tracker with its expected date; the factory that last made it; the cloth; what the health rule suggests.A review is started, which saves today's figures.
B11.P2Is the rate usable?PlanningReads the observed rate (last four in-stock weeks ÷ 4) and enters a planning rate with a reason.The observed rate stays beside the planner's; who changed it and why is kept. A warning when a size was out in the window, and when the option is new.—
B11.P3Stock week by weekSystem—Usable stock projected week by week at the planning rate, with each raised PO added in the week it is expected, not today. A request with no PO yet is shown but not counted. The week a new order placed today would land is marked, with the stock and cover left then.—
B11.P4Size by sizeSystem—The same projection per size: usable now, incoming, rate, stock at arrival, and the first week each size runs out, flagged when that is before any receipt. Sizes reconcile to the option total.—
B11.P5Draft quantityPlanningSets the cover wanted after arrival.Target demand after receipt, less projected stock at arrival, gives the sellable requirement. Display pieces to add and the vendor MOQ show as question marks until confirmed.The planner enters the approved quantity and why it differs from the working.
B11.P6Size ratioPlanningEdits the ratio per size, or scales the draft to the approved quantity.The draft spreads the gap on each size's rate and adds back any size short at arrival; cover after arrival is shown per size. The draft and the approved ratio are both kept.—
B11.P7Approve, then to designPlanningApproves the plan, then sends it to design.Approval needs the display and MOQ questions answered and a ratio that adds up. Sending creates the RTW requirement with the quantity and ratio filled in and the proto skipped as a repeat.Design sends it on; it runs as any RTW style (B8) and shows on the tracker as "no PO yet".
B11.P8Next sittingPlanningOpens the option again.Every past review with its planned rate against actual sales since, the weeks every size was in stock, the size mix against sizes now out, and where the order is. Lost sales are not estimated; the weeks a size was unavailable are flagged.—

The non-core count tab shows live non-core options per category against the plan, how many are still on the shelf in eight weeks at the observed rate, and which will not survive the period with nothing landing in time. Whether to replace one or let the count fall short stays planning's call. Store transfers carry a purpose: display, off the rack, or trial.

Sources: planning-team call relayed 8 Oct 2026; worked example "one RTW option from planning to store availability".

B11-ii. RTW option book — the RTW stock health page, with three tabs: "Every option · what to do", "Option count vs the plan", and "Replenish & pull back · this week". Every option gets one of the workbook's nine action words, checked in the order below, using these measures: stock (all sizes added up); the true rate of sale, which is the average of the last 4 weeks out of 12 when the size set was whole (weeks with sizes broken are dropped); the plain 12-week average, shown beside it as "the number that made this look weak"; the top-6-week rate, the best 6 in-stock weeks divided by 6; weeks of cover, stock divided by the true rate of sale; how many sizes are at zero, and whether a middle size (40, 41, 42, 32, 34, M, L) is out; velocity A at 10 or more a week, B at 5 or more, C at 2 or more, D above zero (0.1 or more), E at zero. Healthy means stock above zero, at most one size out and under 30 weeks of cover; health is Unknown with under 4 weeks of sales; Inactive when taken off the shelf.

VerbWhen (BSC PLM's rule)Workbook's rule where it differsAction offered on the option
REINTRODUCEInactive and top-6-week rate of 2 or moreWorkbook: stock at zero and top-6-week rate of 2 or more (active options)"Make active & order n pcs" · "Make active only"
URGENT REPLENISHActive, stock at zero and top-6-week rate of 2 or more; or Healthy with 3 weeks of cover or lessThe workbook gives stock-at-zero to REINTRODUCE; here REINTRODUCE is kept for options already off the shelf"Raise reorder · n pcs" (n is at least 150, or the suggested quantity if higher)
REVIEWStock at zero, no sign of demandsame"Take off the shelf"
SIZE FILLTwo or more sizes out and still selling (checked after the overstock rules)Workbook priority 2, before URGENT REPLENISH"Raise reorder · n pcs"
LIQUIDATEVelocity E with stock on handsame"Liquidate, pull displays & take off the shelf"
EXIT55 or more weeks of coversame"Liquidate, pull displays & take off the shelf"
CLEAR / DISCOUNT30 to under 55 weeks of coversame"Liquidate, pull displays & take off the shelf"
MAINTAINHealthysame"nothing to decide"; "Promote to core" if non-core
MONITOREverything else; inactive with no demand signalsame"Take off the shelf"

The reorder maths on the option's page ("Sizes, sales and the reorder maths · 90-day trigger"): planned weekly demand is the lower of the true rate of sale × 1.5 and the top-6-week rate — the 1.5 growth bump is BSC's figure for bottoms, and whether shirts take it too is to be confirmed with BSC. Cover is stock plus pieces on order divided by planned demand, in days; the trigger is under 90 days. The suggested reorder is planned demand × (lead time + 90 days) ÷ 7, less stock and pieces on order, rounded up to the minimum order of 150; lead time is the last PO's factory lead, else 40 days. Sell-through is pieces sold ÷ pieces ordered, where ordered is the delivered (or ordered) pieces on POs matched by style name; with no matching PO it falls back to sold ÷ (sold + stock). What the buttons do: "Promote to core" marks the option core; "Raise reorder" adds n to the incoming quantity only; "Make active" puts the option back on the shelf (and adds to incoming when ordered); "Take off the shelf" and "Liquidate" mark it inactive with the date, the person and the note as the reason. The note is kept only as the reason for going inactive. Later: "Raise reorder" will start a new requirement (B5) instead of only adding to incoming, and every decision will leave a line in the option's history. Inactive options keep their history, drop out of the plan counts and show under "Off the shelf".

The plan tab lists each sub-category's ideal total (shirts 35 · 28 · 10 · not set · 10 · 8 · 8 · 10 · 25; bottoms 14 · 5 · 40 · 20), then core ideal, core listed, core healthy, core with a size out, core gap, non-core ideal, non-core listed, healthy and gap; every count opens the list behind it. The replenish tab follows the Replenishment sheet's layout per style and size — At unit · Allocate · Balance · To stores at zero · Pull back — for options marked SIZE FILL, URGENT REPLENISH, CLEAR / DISCOUNT, LIQUIDATE or EXIT: one piece to each store that holds the style and is at zero, within a budget of what a month sells (weekly sales ÷ sizes × 4), and nothing to slow options; pull back from stores holding more than a month's sales (and at least 6 pieces, or any stock of a slow option). The split by store is estimated from the option's total for now; real store-by-store stock needs Enigma's stock-transfer and store data, which BSC PLM cannot read yet. The store's own "Pull back · slow stock" list shows the LIQUIDATE and EXIT options.

Rules. The RTW rate of sale never counts a week when the size set was broken. The MTM reorder point uses the current 8-week rate of sale, not a typed-in weekly average. No PO is raised silently: auto-POs are listed with where they came from, manual ones are clicked by a person, and both need the mill's confirmation before they count as real cover. Core is replenished size by size: a core option with a size out raises a planning alert.

Cases.

CaseWhat differs
MTM core, domestic millListed as an auto-PO; sourcing confirms with the mill; the goods-received note moves the metres from on order to the shelf
MTM core, international mill"Raise PO · n m" on the fabric's page and the "Raise the PO by hand" filter; recorded as raised by hand; lead time from the mill's record
MTM non-coreNever reordered; it runs out; the "Ranges · non-core gaps" tab counts the family against its plan, and a gap raises an option request (B6)
MTM bespokeNever below the point; availability comes from the mill's list ("Bespoke · mill availability"); sold out puts it on hold
Paused fabricLeft off the store's list; still counted on the master, with the reason
