RTW core optionThe full set of action words; URGENT REPLENISH and SIZE FILL offer the reorder
RTW non-core optionThe same action words (URGENT REPLENISH is not held back for non-core); "Promote to core" appears when Healthy
New option (under 4 weeks of sales)Health reads Unknown; the action word is still worked out
Inactive optionShown under "Off the shelf"; REINTRODUCE when the top-6-week rate is 2 or more, else MONITOR
A store asks another store for a sizeCounted as store demand: a "Stores asked for it" filter, a planning alert at 2 or more asks, and the list of asks on the option's page

Time target. An action word on every option whenever the list is opened; a decision clicked within the week it appears (the weekly rhythm of the workbook's Action History tab); a mill check on every core fabric every 7 days; an unconfirmed fabric PO chased after 2 days. To be confirmed with BSC: the target of 30 active fabrics per MTM book, each mill's lead time and minimum order (the vendor master holds neither today), and whether the option-count plan stays monthly.

Sources: Tranbscript of granola recording (Meeting 1 on auto-PO; Meeting 3 on the 90-day trigger, top 6 weeks, sell-through); Neha on the Enigma auto-PO rule (30 Jul 2026); C1a MTM call transcript and summary (27 Aug 2026); Sourcing call transcript (weekly supplier check); RTW shirts inventory call and transcript (12 Aug 2026); Enigma Answers (26 Aug 2026); data-files: RTW_Shirts_Concise_Updated 10.xlsx (Velocity Parameters, Action & Gap Parameters, Action History), ROS Health Report 020826.xlsx, Replenishment Sample.xlsx, Option Count Summary Sample.xlsx.

B12. Target Flow H — Warehouse

Everything that arrives is received piece by piece, or lot by lot, against what was declared. Every receipt writes one numbered goods-received note (GRN-26-nnnn), and that note reaches finance with its PO. The warehouse's page has three groups:

Two asks that come from the stores. A short count on a stock transfer (the store counted in fewer than the STO said): the warehouse sends the balance or writes the gap off with a reason, and nothing stays open as ghost stock. A swatch-book ask (a store wants a particular book, or more copies): the warehouse dispatches or declines it from the swatch-book list, with the store's reason in front of it.

  • Coming in. Every receipt raises a goods-received note. It covers:

    • the vehicle round (finished made-to-measure shirts coming back)

    • bespoke fabric (mill stock and parcels)

    • fabric rolls against fabric POs

    • ready-to-wear bulk goods against production POs

    • the register of goods-received notes

  • Going out.

    • cut fabric and bundles to the factories

    • approved extra fabric, picked and sent

    • parcels to stores

  • Stores.

    • stock transfers to stores

    • swatch books and fit samples for every store

    • alterations through the hemming unit

Finance has its own view of the same register.

#StepWhoWhat they doWhat they fill inWhat the system checksWhat happens nextTime target
B12.1Vehicle round from a made-to-measure factoryWarehouseOpens the vehicle round. For each factory it shows pieces ready (out of the factory), declared today, received today and the gap. Presses "Receive the round" and marks each piece "Received", "Not on vehicle" or "Turned back", or uses "All received" or "None came". Ends with "Close the round". A single ready piece can also be received on its own.Vehicle and driver (these go on the note), a note for the round, and for any piece turned back, the reason: stitching fault, wrong fabric, measurement off the sheet, stain or damage, wrong customer tag, or missing trims.A closed round is final. Corrections go on the next round.Received pieces are scanned as ready to ship. One goods-received note is written per factory, for example "round · Mahendra, 12 finished MTM pieces", with the vehicle and note in its remarks. A piece not on the vehicle is marked "missed the last vehicle" on the next round. A piece turned back goes back to "in the factory", with the reason, date and who turned it back recorded and added to the note. The factory merchandiser sees the piece back on the floor. Shipping sees the ready pieces on its table.Within the hour

On "Send request", the request date is recorded on the orders. On inward, the order is marked received and moves to planning. A bespoke-parcel goods-received note is written for the order's metres. Planning can now plan the order.Mill list daily; inward on arrival
B12.5Register of goods-received notesWarehouse and financeReads the register, which finance also sees. Each note shows its number, date received, and what it is: fabric rolls, ready-to-wear bulk, bespoke parcel, made-to-measure pieces back, trims and packaging, or counted in at a store. It also shows the PO or code received against, from and to, quantity, rejected quantity, who counted, finance status and invoice. It can be filtered by kind. Finance presses "Pick up · GST & recon".The invoice number, typed by the warehouse or finance on fabric, ready-to-wear and trims notes.No invoice is expected on made-to-measure rounds, bespoke parcels or store receipts. Finance picks up only notes that carry a PO.The invoice number is saved. The note is marked as seen by finance, with the date. Finance's alert counts the notes not yet picked up.Picked up the same week
B12.6Bundles to factoriesWarehouseOpens cut fabric and send bundles. It shows the day's planned made-to-measure orders grouped by plan, with the cut list per fabric (metres and pieces). The warehouse prints the list, then scans the bundles out.Nothing beyond the scan.None.The orders move to in transit. At the scan, the orders' metres come off the shelf and off the held-back total, just as Enigma deducts at its in-production scan. The factory merchandiser scans the bundles in.Daily
B12.7Extra fabricWarehouseOpens extra fabric. Each approved ask shows the panel, the metres and whose error it was. Presses "Picked & sent".Nothing beyond the press.Only approved asks are listed. The production unit approves the ask, and approval is when the metres are deducted.The ask is marked sent. The factory gets it on the next vehicle.Next vehicle
B12.8Parcels to storesWarehouseOpens parcels to stores. For each store it shows what is ready: stock transfers, made-to-measure shirts ready, ready-to-wear pieces, launch pieces and books. The warehouse marks them dispatched.Nothing beyond the dispatch.None.Each item is marked dispatched. The store sees everything under "Received from the unit", ready to count in.Daily
B12.9Stock transfersWarehouse and storeOpens stock transfers. Each row shows the transfer number, type (display, off the rack or replenishment), the store, style, quantity, hemming type, date shipped, date arrived and status: in transit, open n days after arrival, or closed. Presses "Close on receipt". The store's own "count in" also closes it.Nothing beyond the press.Transfers come from the tracker's transfer numbers today. There is no way to create a new transfer here, and launch dispatches are not transfers. Later: issue one transfer per store per launch wave.The warehouse's close records the close date. The store's count-in records the arrival and close dates and writes a store goods-received note. The warehouse is alerted to any transfer shipped more than 7 days ago and not counted in: "this is the gap ghost stock lives in".Counted in within 24 hours of arrival
B12.10Swatch books and fit samplesWarehouse and storeOpens swatch books and fit samples. A grid shows each store against the made-to-measure book, the ready-to-wear book and the bespoke book (which comes from the mill), marked never sent, sent, unconfirmed after 7 days, or received. Presses "Send 1", or "Send 2" for the larger stores (Pali Hill, Select City Walk, Khan Market, Hyderabad, Whitefield, Vasant Kunj and Noida). Uses "Resend" and "Mill has sent" as needed. For the made-to-measure fit set (slim, standard and heavy, in sizes 38 to 44), the gaps are shown with "Send n".Nothing beyond the press.None.Each book is recorded with its kind, edition (AW26), number of copies, sender, dispatch date and tracking number. Each fit sample is recorded with its fit, size, dispatch date and condition (fine, worn or missing). The store confirms receipt from its own list.Confirmed within 7 days
B12.11Alterations through the hemming unitWarehouse, production unit and storeOpens alterations through the hemming unit. It lists every open case going through the unit, with the whole round trip:<br>1. handed to the vehicle by the store<br>2. received at the warehouse<br>3. scanned in at the hemming unit<br>4. hemmed and scanned out<br>5. back at the warehouse<br>6. sent to the store or straight to the customer<br>7. delivered or received<br><br>Whoever holds the piece presses the one button for their hand-off.Where it goes back to (the store or the customer), chosen by the store when it hands the piece over.Each hand-off only after the one before it.Each hand-off is dated on the case. When it is sent back, the case moves to "back at store". The store sees where the trouser is without having to ask.Round trip within the case's promise
B12.12Ready-to-wear customer orders from stockWarehouseReady-to-wear sells like a web shop: if the size is in stock it can be sold, if not it cannot, and no factory is involved. Opens ready-to-wear orders. It lists every order the website or a store punched that is not being handed over from that store's own shelf, with style, size, customer, where it goes (customer address or store), inseam if the piece needs hemming, and promised date. The warehouse picks the piece, sends it through the hemming bench if an inseam is set, packs it, and presses "Dispatched" with the courier and tracking number.Courier and tracking number; a note if the piece was swapped for the same size from another shelf.An order exists only for a style and size with stock at the unit or a store: the website and the store bill both refuse a size that is out, so there are no back orders. Stock leaves the shelf count at punch, not at dispatch. Dispatch only after hemming when an inseam is set.The order moves punched (hemming) dispatched delivered. A store order shows "Handed to customer" once the parcel reaches the store; a website order is delivered by the courier. The style's stock at the unit drops; when the last size at the unit goes, the replenishment rule (B11) is what brings more, never this order.Dispatched same day; 3 days with hemming

Rules.

  • A goods-received note is written by the receipt, never typed by hand. Every kind except store receipts carries the PO or parcel code it was received against.

  • A round is closed once. Corrections are new receipts.

  • Declared against received shows the moment the round closes.

  • When a store writes off a piece, it leaves the shelf count the same minute. The reasons are damaged, soiled, display worn, lost, or wrong item received. The piece is either sent back to the unit or destroyed.

  • Ready-to-wear customer orders are the warehouse's: pick from stock, hem if needed, dispatch. Out of stock means not for sale, not a factory order.

Cases.

CaseWhat happens
A lot short of the POThe balance and its promised date stay on the PO. Closing short is a recorded decision, and it counts as an in-full miss on the on-time-in-full measure.
Rejected metres or piecesRecorded on the goods-received note and on the lot. Later: the fabric note carries a rejected quantity too.
A piece not on the vehicle for three roundsIt keeps "missed the last vehicle". Later: raise it to production after three rounds.
A transfer that arrives at the wrong storeThe receiving store cannot count in another store's transfer. Later: re-route it with a new transfer.
A bespoke parcel with an unknown codeRefused with a message. Later: a list of unmatched parcels for sourcing.
A mill list with a new articleThe line is ignored ("Nothing matched"). Later: create a bespoke fabric that waits for its Enigma code.

Time targets.

  • Vehicle round: closed within one hour of arrival.

  • Fabric goods-received note: the same day.

  • Ready-to-wear lot: the same day.

  • Stock transfer: counted in by the store within 24 hours. The alert fires at 7 days.

  • Swatch book: confirmed within 7 days.

  • Goods-received note: finance picks it up in the week it is written.

Sources: store visit transcript (stock transfers, ghost stock); Enigma Answers (26 Aug 2026) (deduction at the in-production scan); Discovery Record — What We Know v1.0 (18 Aug 2026); the STO tracker in data-files.

B12a. Target Flow I — MTM daily production

The production unit runs its day from one MTM daily production screen with seven tabs: Allocate, Plans, Factory floor, Changed sheets, Extra fabric, Daily output and Aging. The warehouse works from its own lists for cutting and sending bundles and for the vehicle round. Each factory's merchandiser works from their own factory screen. Every move of a piece is a scan. The system proposes the plan from each factory's spare capacity, and a person confirms it. If a sheet changes after the bundle has left, the factory gets an amended version flagged for reprint, not a WhatsApp message.

flowchart LR
  A[Order punched<br/>metres reserved] --> B[Freeze window<br/>a setting, 2 hours by default]
  B --> C[Ready queue<br/>urgent first, then oldest punch first]
  C --> D[Proposal per factory<br/>from spare capacity]
  D --> E[Plan submitted<br/>plan number, job cards, cut list]
  E --> F[Scanned out: in production<br/>metres deducted] --> G[Scanned in at the factory]
  G --> H[Ready: out of the factory] --> I[Vehicle round: ready to ship and GRN]
  I --> J[Dispatch] --> K[Delivered]
#StepWhoWhat they doWhat they fill inWhat the system checksWhat happens nextTime target
B12a.1QueueSystemNothing to click. The Allocate tab shows the ready queue: urgent orders first, then by punch time. Orders punched in the last 2 hours are folded away as held back ("stores still change them"), with the time each will join the plan. The planner can change the freeze window on the tabFreeze window in hours (0 to 24, in half-hours)An order joins the queue only once it is older than the freeze window. Bespoke orders stay out until their parcel of cloth has been receivedNothing is recorded yetContinuous
B12a.2ProposalSystemNothing to click. The Allocate tab shows one card per MTM factory (Mahendra, Richman, Harsh). Each card shows daily feed, pieces in the factory and their age, pieces in transit, spare capacity today, load in days and how many orders are proposed. The queue shows each order's proposed factory and the reason—Corporate orders go to the corporate factory (Mahendra). Urgent orders go to the urgent-reserve factory (Richman) if it has room, otherwise the nearest factory with room. Normal orders go to the factory with the most spare capacity, oldest first. Spare capacity is the daily capacity, minus pieces in transit, minus any pieces in the factory beyond two days' work. Daily capacity is the factory's daily target, or its average output over the last 7 days. An order that cannot be placed says "no factory has headroom today"Nothing is recorded until the planner submitsSeconds
B12a.3Planner confirmsProduction plannerPick the orders: all shown, the oldest 20, 30 or 50, a number by punch time, or a card's proposed orders. Factory. "Send anyway" to go over capacityIf the plan is over the factory's spare capacity, it is refused ("room for N today. B12a.6Sheet change after planningStore, then production, then factoryFrom the order sheet (on the store, production and warehouse screens), click a field, fill in the correction and why, and click "Save correction" or "Amend — re-issue to factory". Fabric, fit block, tag or remarks can be changed. Once a factory is allocated, the store can only use "Add a remark". Production reads each change on the changed sheets tab and clicks "Read — plan on this" (not yet allocated) or "Told <factory>" (allocated). The factory reads the changes under "Changed after it reached us" and clicks "Read". "Re-issue to factory" clears the reprint flag. A fabric, tag or quantity change after planning warns that the order "may belong at a different factory". Production can then click "Pull back to PP"The corrected value and whyStores can change anything freely until a factory is allocated. After that, and until the piece leaves the factory, they can only add remarks. Production can change anything freely up to planning. While the piece is in transit or in the factory, changes become amendments flagged for reprint. From out of the factory on, the sheet is closed to changes. A fabric change moves the reserved metres to the new fabric, unless the cloth has already been cutEach change is kept as a numbered revision: who, their role, when, the note, each field from and to, and whether it needs a reprint. The system records when production read it, when the factory was told and when the factory read it. Pulling an order back to PP clears its factory and plan and logs a revision ("planned → back in PP"). Production's tab counts changes "pending with me" and "pending with the factory". The warehouse picks the cloth and clicks "Picked & sent"Order number (grouped by customer, store and day). Which cloth, if the bill has several. Panel: single front 0.8 m, both fronts (patta) 0.9 m, single sleeve 0.8 m, both sleeves 0.9 m, collar 0.3 m, cuff 0.3 m. Metres (can be changed). Reason: damaged or yarn pull (factory error), stain (factory error), weaving defect (PP error), fabric short (PP error), shade mismatch (PP error). NoteThe order must have a fabric and a factory. Metres are not capped per panel. Whether they should be capped at the panel allowance is to be confirmed with BSCThe request is recorded as requested, then approved or declined, then sent, with who raised it, when, and when it was decided. Approving deducts the metres from fabric stock at once, because approval is the stock event. Each team is told in turn. Factory errors are meant for the vendor's record and PP errors for the consumption rule. Later: count these per vendor and feed them into the consumption ruleSame day

B12a.9Shipping and deliveryProduction or shippingOn the Plans tab, click "Dispatch · N" to dispatch the ready pieces. Then click "Delivered" on the order sheet. Click "Close plan" once every piece is ready, dispatched or deliveredNothingA plan can be closed only when every piece has reached ready to ship or beyondDispatch and delivery are scanned, and the plan's close date is recorded. Cancelling an order gives its reserved metres back only if the cloth has not been cut. The store sees it on its outbound listBy the ship-by date
B12a.10Aging and outputSystem (production sets factory capacity)The Aging tab shows pieces past ship-by, due today, or all open. It also shows factory by stage, days in the factory (0–2, 3–5, 6–8 and 9+ days, oldest per factory, over 5 days meaning "stuck, not slow") and ship-by date by factory. The Daily output tab shows, per factory: tailors, capacity per day, average per day, out today (from scans), the factory's own figure, in the factory, aging, in transit and load. Production edits the capacity per day hereCapacity per day, per factoryNothingThe factory's capacity is saved. Aging is shown on screen only. No one is told when a piece passes 5 days. Later: alert production when a piece passes 5 days in a factoryLive

Rules. The freeze window is a setting, 2 hours by default, not a habit. A plan is submitted once. Going over capacity is recorded with the name of who forced it. Pulling an order back to PP is logged as a revision. Each factory's capacity is its daily target, edited on the Daily output tab. The standard capacity is tailors × 12 pieces a day. From out of the factory on, a sheet is closed to changes: any change then is an alteration or a remake, handled as a return. The factory sees a reprint flag and the changed fields outlined, never a WhatsApp. Fabric on the sheet is the base (1.6 m, or 1.85 m for linen) plus a 0.10 m buffer. It is reserved when the order is punched and deducted when it is scanned out. The 16 Sep alignment asked for: select 30, 40 or 50; filters for urgent, VIP, corporate and normal; a capacity-versus-output refresh about every 10 minutes; statuses "Order Modified" and "Order Modified: Pending"; and changes locked at PP2. The VIP tag, the 10-minute refresh and the "Order Modified" status names are still to come (later: add them as agreed on 16 Sep).

Cases.

CaseWhat differs
Urgent tagFirst in the queue, with a ship-by date of punch + 2 days. Proposed to the urgent-reserve factory, or the nearest with room. Still held for the freeze window unless someone changes the setting
Corporate order (many pieces, one customer)Always proposed to the corporate factory. One bill, with each piece as its own order, and one plan
Bespoke fabricKept out of the queue until the customer's parcel of cloth is received. Receiving it books a bespoke-parcel GRN and moves the order to PP. The Allocate table shows "bespoke <code>"
Fabric short at cuttingThere is no status for this, and the unit raises nothing. Later: pull the order back to PP with a reason
Sheet change before planningThe store and unit change it freely. It sits on production's "pending with me" list until they click "Read — plan on this"
Sheet change after the bundle has leftThe unit amends it with a reprint flag while the piece is in transit or in the factory. The store can only add a remark. It sits on the factory's "Changed since it arrived" list until they click "Read"
Turned back at the unitGoes back to "in the factory" with the reason and is counted again in the factory's ready list. The row shows "turned back ×N"
Piece missed the vehicleMarked "Not on vehicle", shown as "missed the last vehicle", and stays out of the factory for the next round
Customer cancelsThe reserved metres are given back if the cloth has not been cut. After the scan-out nothing is put back ("releasing it again would invent metres")
RTW bottoms with inseamNot MTM. RTW orders go from punched to ready to hemming to delivered, and never through a factory plan

Time targets. Punch to plan: the next planning run after the freeze window. Plan to factory scan-in: 1 day. In the factory: 3 days (5 days counts as aging). Out of the factory to ready to ship: the same day's vehicle. Ship-by on the record: punch + 7 days, or + 2 days for urgent. Earlier notes gave 14 days from punch to delivery, but no record backs this. To be confirmed with BSC.

Sources: Granola notes "BSC Production MTM - Alignment" (16 Sep 2026); BSC MTM factory list (Mahendra, Richman, Harsh).

B12b. Target Flow J — RTW production: milestones and final steps

This is section 4 of the RTW record: the time-and-action milestones and final steps. Nitin and the factory merchandiser keep it up to date, and the merchandiser does it in real time from their portal. The tracker lists open POs, closed POs and RTW customer orders, filtered by Needs attention, Delayed, Critical, Watch, On track and "Before the PO". A separate RTW production analytics screen, for the RTW planner, carries delivery performance, the daily monitor, escalations and alerts.

Rules. Plan dates are never overwritten: a revision sits beside the plan date and an actual replaces the revision. On time in full is judged on the whole order, within the tolerance set in the tracker's settings.

Cases. Repeat style: the sample section can be skipped. Factory change mid-run: a new proposal and a new factory PO redraw the plan. A milestone late with no revision reads watch until someone enters the actual or the reason. A lot held by QC is shown and blocks nothing yet. A partial delivery keeps the PO open with its balance.

Sources: call with Nitin, 3 Oct 2026; Production Tracker Final V2.xlsx (milestones, Config, Auto Plan Logic, risk thresholds, OTIF tolerance, delay reasons, OTIF Dashboard); Transcript of call (Parag, 33:59–35:49).

B12c. Analytics — what replaces the workbooks

Three workbooks become one sales review page: the weekly business review, the daily sales report (DSR) and the stylist KPI sheet. The page has tabs for the weekly review, stores and DSR, and stylists and KPI. It reads from the same rows that day-to-day operations run on. For every figure, a "How these numbers are made" table on the page names the source workbook and column.

For now, the figures are the reports' own. They are lifted from the workbook files for the week ending Sunday 30 Aug 2026, and the page shows them as the reports state them without recalculating. Finance workbooks stay with finance. The ROS and health report has its own page (stock health, B11).

Today's workbookTargetWhereHow the number is made
Product Weekly Report · Summary tabWeekly review, "The week in one table". For each segment: last week's quantity and revenue, the week before's quantity and revenue, the change in each, and the four-week average quantity and revenue. Share rows show as percentages, and a move of more than 2% either way is coloured.Sales review, used by planning; design and leadership can read itLast week, the week before, and the change (last week minus the week before, divided by the week before), as the summary block states them. A blank segment stays blank, never zero. It replaces the sheet's columns for last week and the week before (quantity, revenue and % change) and the four-week average.
Product Weekly Report · last-four-weeks tabWeekly review, "Category × sub-category". Columns: share, average weekly sales over 12 weeks and over the last 4, warehouse stock, each of the last four weeks, the best week (its value and which week it was), and last week against the four-week average. Warehouse stock turns amber when it would last more than 30 weeks at the four-week rate; hovering shows the weeks. A switch shows units or revenue.SameShare, 12-week average weekly sales, 4-week average weekly sales and warehouse stock are taken from the sheet's own columns, not recalculated. The four weeks are shown in the sheet's order, each with units and value. The best week is the largest of the four weeks on the row. Last week against the average is last week minus the mean of the non-blank weeks, divided by that mean. Over-stock means warehouse stock divided by the 4-week average weekly sales is above 30. Weeks with a broken size set are not removed from the average; the sheet's figure is shown as it stands.
Product Weekly Report · MTM shirt range tab and ready-to-wear sub-category tabsWeekly review, "By range". Ready-to-wear shirts by sub-category (9 ranges plus a grand total), or made-to-measure by fabric range (57 ranges). Columns: 12-week average quantity, each of the last four weeks, the best week, quantity against the week before, and quantity against the 12-week average. Ranges with no sales in four weeks are hidden. Sorted by last week.SameThe 12-week average and the two growth columns are the sheet's own whole percentages. To be fixed: the range tables currently show revenue where units are expected. The range sheets list revenue first and quantity second for each week, but the page reads the first as units, so the units view shows revenue and the sort follows revenue. Check: 100% linen, four weeks back, is ₹507,072 and 80 pieces on the 9 Aug sheet.

Product On Sale (hidden tab)Weekly review, "On sale". Style (with category), price (MRP struck through, sale price and % off), date moved to sale, stock before, rate of sale before, rate of sale after (green when higher), and the ten weeks, newest first. Filters: every style, discount moved it, discount did nothing. An export. A footer reads "n of m markdowns lifted the rate of sale; the rest have a stock problem, not a price problem".SameTaken from the sheet's columns: original MRP, new discounted price, date moving to sale, stock before going to sale, pre-sale rate of sale, post-sale four-week rate of sale, and the first ten weekly columns (15–21 June back to 13–19 April). "Moved it" means the rate after the sale is higher than before, both as the sheet worked them out.
DSRStores and DSR. For each store: city and region (marked "new store" if it is not like-for-like), the month's target, month-to-date sales against the pro-rated target (green at 100% or more, amber at 85% or more, red below), the projected month (and its % of target), growth on last month and on last year, average order value, new against returning (a share bar with bill counts), and new customers against target. Filters: region, every store, like-for-like stores or new stores, and a search. A totals row and an export.Retail operations. Later: store managers see only their own store.Month-to-date sales and target, achievement %, projected month, growth on last month and last year, month-to-date average order value, new and returning sales, bills and customers, and the new-customer target and achievement, all as the sheet states them. Only the 35 rows that have a region and are not totals count as stores. The store and online split, region and grand-total rows are left out. New share is new sales divided by new plus returning sales. The totals row adds up the filtered stores and works out the % of pro-rated target again.
Stylist KPIStylists and KPI. Every stylist (281, with store totals left out), or store totals. Columns: target ("no target" when zero), revenue and achievement (green at 100% or more, amber at 80% or more), bills and pieces, average selling price, average order value, units per order, new and returning with their average order values, and NPS ("—" where it was not captured). A store filter and an export.Retail operationsEach row as the workbook states it. Rows marked "Store Total" are kept out of the people list. Achievement shows only when there is a target. Conversion, call target, calls done and NPS are empty on every row in the file we have.
ROS & Health, ConciseThe planning decisions on stock health (B11-ii), the plan cells, and the history of actions on every style's record.PlanningWorked out each night from Enigma stock and Shopify sales (B11).
Option Count SummaryPlan cells on stock health: number of styles per sub-category against the ideal (overall and for core). New, in production and in planning are counted from requirements and launches.Planning, designCounted from where each requirement stands and from launches, not typed in.
AgeingStock health: age buckets per style, worked out from receipt dates, and a list of styles to consider for liquidation.PlanningFrom the receipt dates on goods-received notes, and from stock. The buckets follow the sheet: 0–90, 90–120, 120–180, 180–270 and over 270 days. This is to be confirmed with BSC, because the July sample has no 90–120 bucket.
Fit & Display Projection; Display & Fit Sample AllocationLaunches: pieces per store. The store confirms receipt (B10-iv), and cost lines are hidden from the store.Planning, warehouseFrom the launch record.
Replenishment samplePhase 5: proposed stock transfers per store, based on each store's rate of sale and weeks of cover.PlanningOutside phases 1 to 4.
Margin working, Category penetration, Inventory value, Working capitalThese stay in finance's sheets. The tool exports the rows they need: sales by product code, stock by style, and receipts.FinanceOut of scope.

Rules.

  • Every number explains itself. Each figure has a "how this is made" note. The note gives the source workbook and column, the calculation, the period, the scope, and what happens with missing or incomplete data. Later: each figure links to the rows behind it.

  • Reports are pages, not attachments. The Monday mail carries a link.

  • Figures freeze at the week's end. Every figure is frozen at the end of Sunday, the day the reports are dated, so the review and the records agree. Saturday midnight does not match the files.

  • Blank is blank, never zero.

  • The page says what the workbooks cannot answer yet. They carry four weeks of detail plus a 12-week average. So "best week out of twelve" and "last six weeks against the six before" cannot be worked out from them. Either the extract keeps twelve weekly columns, or the review reads order lines by style code and store code.

  • Definitions are signed before any recalculation. BSC must sign off rate of sale, sell-through, like-for-like growth, repeat customer and the other close-out definitions. Until then, figures are shown as the workbooks state them, not worked out again.

Time target. The Monday workbook effort goes to zero; today it takes one person about half a day, to be confirmed with BSC. The review is read live. The DSR morning call reads month-to-date sales against the pro-rated target straight from the page.

Sources: Product Weekly Report 090826 2.xlsx (column order of the MTM shirt range and Product On Sale tabs); Ageing 01.07.2026 AMAN AGEING Sample Done.xlsx (bucket names); the DSR and Stylist KPI workbooks in data-files; Discovery Close-Out — 4-Day Field Plan (07 Sep 2026), section 3.

B12d. Target Flow L — Bespoke, mill to customer

Bespoke cloth is never on BSC's shelf. The mill holds it, reports what it has, and cuts a length against each customer order. So the flow has one daily job (the mill's list), one gate (a fabric the mill no longer has cannot be sold), and one handshake (the parcel carries a code BSC minted, so it matches its order by itself). Everything after the cloth lands is ordinary made-to-measure production (section 10).

#StepWhoWhat they doWhat they fill inWhat the system checksWhat happens nextTime target
B12d.1The mill's list, every dayWarehouse (today: whoever retypes the five emails)Opens bespoke, picks a mill, types the metres the mill reported against each fabric and clicks "Save" — or pastes the mill's own list and clicks "Apply". A fabric the mill says is gone gets "Out".Metres per fabric. In a pasted list: the fabric's code or name and a number, or "out", "OOS", "nil".A fabric reported at zero or out goes on hold the same second — off the website and out of every store's pick list. A fabric reported back in stock comes off hold. Rows the mill did not mention are marked reported today, unchanged.The mill's metres, availability and report date sit on the fabric. Stores see today's stock. A mill not reported today shows as due on the warehouse's list and on their Today.Every mill before 10:00; a list older than a day reads "due"
B12d.2The customer ordersStore associatePunches the bill as for any made-to-measure shirt; the fabric picker offers bespoke fabrics from the books.Customer, fabric, fit, measurements, promised date.A bespoke fabric can be picked only if it is live: not paused, not on hold, and the mill's last report said available. The store cannot sell what the mill does not have.The order is minted a parcel code (the mill's prefix plus a short code). It waits for cloth: planning does not see it until the parcel is in. Today Enigma raises a per-order PO in a silent batch; here the call to the mill (B12d.3) is that PO.At punch; promised date to confirm with BSC (longer than the 14-day made-to-measure default)
B12d.3Call the clothWarehouseOpens "Call from the mills", which groups the day's new bespoke orders by mill with their codes and metres, and clicks "Send request" per mill.Nothing beyond the click; the request carries the codes and metres.One request per mill per day; an order punched later joins the next day's.The request date goes on each order; they move to "awaited". The mill sticks BSC's code on the parcel. An order awaited more than 10 days turns red on the list and on the warehouse's Today.Same day as the order
B12d.4The parcel arrivesWarehouseScans or types the parcel's code and clicks "Inward".The code.The code must match an open bespoke order not yet received; otherwise "No open bespoke order carries code …" and nothing moves. B12d.6The books in storesWarehouse and storeOn the swatch-books grid, the bespoke book is the mill's own; the warehouse marks "Mill has sent", the store confirms it arrived. A fabric the mill has dropped is switched off here, and the store is told to sticker the swatch.—A book unconfirmed after 7 days shows on both lists.Every store's bespoke book is counted, which it is not today.Confirmed within 7 days
B12d.7Reading itPlanningThe weekly review's bespoke tab reads off the same orders: bespoke against core and non-core by ticket band, each of the last four weeks (section 12).———Monday

Rules. Bespoke has no reorder point and never fires a stock purchase order: the only thing that makes a bespoke fabric sellable is the mill's last report. Sold out puts it on hold at once, everywhere, with no second person remembering to do it. The parcel's code is minted by BSC when the order is punched, never typed in from the mill's paperwork, so the match is exact. A bespoke order waits outside the plan until its cloth is in. The goods-received note is for the order's metres only; the fabric never joins BSC's shelf stock. Who owns bespoke at BSC is still to be confirmed — the flow above gives the daily list and the parcels to the warehouse and the books to the warehouse and stores, and leaves the mill relationship with sourcing.

Cases.

CaseWhat happensWhy
The mill reports only available or unavailable (Thomas Mason, Albini)Metres stay blank; availability alone switches the fabric on or offThese mills give no quantity; the gate still holds
The mill sells out after the order was punchedThe fabric goes on hold; the order stays awaited and turns red at 10 days; the store is told and the customer chooses another cloth or a refund through the store's case ladder (section 6)To build: today nobody measures how often this happens
A parcel arrives with a code no open order carriesRefused at inward with the message; the parcel waitsLater: a holding list
The mill's list did not come todayThe fabric shows "due"; yesterday's metres stand and the fabric stays sellableA missing report is not a sell-out
A bespoke article sells so well that BSC wants to stock itDesign promotes it to core from the fabric master, which gives it a reorder point and asks sourcing for a supply block (section 1 and 7)Open question Q81: has this ever happened
A customer orders a bespoke fabric onlineSame flow; the parcel code and the wait are the same; the promised date shown online must come from the mill's lead timeTo confirm with BSC

Time targets. Mill list before 10:00 every day; the call to the mill the same day the order is punched; the parcel inside the mill's lead time (to confirm per mill: a week or so domestic, about three weeks from Italy); inward the day it lands; a bespoke book confirmed within 7 days of dispatch.

Sources: current-process section A13 and its sources; the MTM production and warehouse flows (B12.4 bespoke parcels, B12.10 swatch books, B12a bespoke gate, B11 "never below the point"); Enigma Answers finding 2 (per-order bespoke PO, batch times, missing vendor codes); Question Bank block F; Thinking Doc — MTM First Cut, Phase 2 ("bespoke availability ingested instead of re-keyed").

B13. Business rules and validations

Every guard the store enforces, action by action, with the toast the user sees. Two kinds of refusal exist in the model: a silent return inside touch() (nothing is written, no message — the screen usually hides the button instead), and an explicit warning toast (tone: 'warn') with no write. Toasts marked (undo) carry an Undo button for 7 seconds. Rules the earlier text described that the code does not enforce are marked "(not in the model yet — to build)".

ActionRule / guard in the codeText the user sees
Create requirement (RTW/FOB)Form-level only; status Requirement, BOM and costing defaulted from the category, costingClosure.plan = today + 10 d"Requirement created. It is now sourcing's turn."
Update requirementOnly while status === 'Requirement' (screen gate)"Saved."
Hand to sourcingSets Quoting, resets sourcing"Handed to sourcing. They will come back with vendors."
BOM saved—"BOM saved."
Add vendor to a style (addFabricQuote)One live vendor: the unchosen quote is dropped and logged "Vendor replaced · {mill} withdrawn by sourcing"; status Requirement → Quoting"Vendor added with its terms."
Remove vendorRefused if the quote is selected; last one resets stage to vendors"Vendor removed."
Send to designRefused with no quotes"Sent to design. It is their turn to choose."
Ask extension (style)Records who asked (design or planning)"Extension asked. Sourcing will go back to the mills." (warn)
Extend a holdNew date typed (default today + 14 d); no check that it is later than the old one (to build)"Hold extended."
Send back to designResolves the extension"Back with design."

B14. Alert engine

One bell per person (MentionBar, pane "To act on"), showing only their team's items; every line opens the record at the step. The list is alertsFor(db, myRole, { storeId }), recomputed on every render from the records — nothing is stored, nothing is emailed, and there is no nightly job: a line disappears the moment the record moves. Lines are sorted by weight descending (highest cost of ignoring first), not oldest first. The Owner sees every role's lines. Level is critical · warn · info. The rules, in code order:

Rule (id pattern)Fires forWhenTitle / detail (exact pattern)Lands onLevel · weight
po-{orderId}productionAn Open PO with risk critical or delayed"{PO no. or 'PO'} · {style}" / "{n}d past the target inhouse date · {qty} pcs" or "buffer down to {n}d · {qty} pcs"#/tracker?open={id}critical if delayed else warn · 1000 − buffer
blk-{blockId}sourcingA held block with ≤ 21 days left (negative = lapsed)"{fabric} · block expiring" / "{n}d left · {m} m held"; lapsed: "{fabric} · block expired" / "lapsed {n}d ago · {m} m released back to the mill"#/fabrics?tab=blockscritical if ≤ 7 d else warn · 900 − left
chk-{fabricId}sourcingCore fabric with no supply review, or reviewed ≥ 7 days ago (worst 8)"{fabric} · mill check due" / "last asked {n}d ago" or "never asked"#/fabrics?tab=coreinfo · 300
fab-choose-{orderId}designNominated style in Quoting/Allocated whose state is choose"{style} · {n} fabric option(s) to choose from" / "sourcing submitted {n}d ago" · "the mill's hold lapsed {n}d ago — ask sourcing to extend" · "the first hold lapses in {n}d"#/design?open={id}critical if hold ≤ 3 d else warn · 800 − holdDays (30 if none)
fab-extend-{orderId}sourcingState extend"{style} · design asked for an extension" / the extension note or "go back to the mills and extend the hold"#/sourcing?open={id}critical · 850
fab-vendors-{orderId}sourcingState vendors and handed over ≥ 3 days ago"{style} · options not yet sent to design" or "… · no vendor asked yet" / "handed over {n}d ago · fabric needed by {date}" (target in-house − 35 d)#/sourcing?open={id}warn · 500 + days
fab-po-{orderId}designState raise-po (chosen, no PO)"{style} · design to push PO" / "{metres or —} m from {mill}"#/design?open={id}warn · 700
sw-cut-{conceptId}warehouseNew-fabric concept not live, swatch marked priority and not cut"{concept} · priority swatch not cut" / "with the swatch team since {date}" · "inwarded {date} · not yet handed over" · "marked priority"#/swatches?open={id}warn · 560
sw-ho-{conceptId}warehouseInwarded ≥ 2 days, not handed to the swatch team (when not priority)"{concept} · cloth in {n}d, not with the swatch team" / "hand it over so the 25 × 25s can be cut"samewarn · 520
sw-tell-{conceptId}designDispatched, stores not told"{concept} · swatches out, stores not told" / "dispatched {date}[ · photos shared] — tell the stores and set it live"samewarn · 600

orqx-{optionId}sourcingOption slot extension"{REQ} · option {n} — extension asked" / the notesamecritical · 850
orqo-{reqId}sourcingA request with open slots, raised ≥ 3 days ago (one line per request)"{REQ} · {category} — sourcing to add vendors on {n} option(s)" / "option(s) {indexes} · asked {n}d ago[ · live by {liveBy}]"#/design?open={reqId}&slot={firstOpen}warn · 480 + days
cfo-{conceptId}designConcept with offers sent and no fabric chosen"{concept} · {n} mill(s) quoted — choose the cloth" / "from ₹{first buy} for the first buy" or "priced", then " · a hold lapsed {n}d ago" or " · first hold lapses in {n}d"#/design?open={id}critical if ≤ 5 d else warn · 820 − holdDays
cn-{conceptId}designA PP or shipment round with the production unit for > 5 days"{concept} · sample waiting on you" / "{n} days on the desk"#/design?open={id}warn if > 10 d else info · 500 + days
lnb-{launchId}planningMarketing brief due (launch − 10 d) passed, not sent"{style} · marketing brief late" / "the Shopify listing is blocked until the name is sent"#/launches?open={id}warn · 700
lnl-{launchId}planningLaunch date passed, not launched"{style} · launch date passed" / "{n}d past the date, not made live"samecritical · 950
opt-{optionId}planningCore option with ≥ 1 size at zero (worst 10)"{style} · core, {n} size(s) out" / "{ros} a week and nothing in those sizes to sell"#/health?open={id}warn if ≥ 2 sizes else info · 600 + 10 × sizes
dmd-{optionId}planning≥ 2 store asks for one option (top 8)"{style} · {n} stores have asked for it" / "sizes {list} · [{k} still unanswered — ]the shelf that should hold it does not"#/health?open={id}warn if unanswered else info · 680 + 5 × asks
ret-{caseId}store (own store)Open case past its SLA (10 d)"{case no.} · {style}" / "{n}d open against a {sla}d promise · {status label}"#/stores?tab=returns&open={id}warn · 650
inbstoreParcels (sto / mtm / rtw) on the way, not counted in"{n} parcel(s) to count in" / "arrived from the unit and not yet counted — until they are, the shelf and the system disagree"#/stores?tab=inwardinfo · 400
req-{requestId}store (the store that has it)A stock ask in requested"{style} · {size} asked for" / "another store needs it — accept, ship or decline"#/stores?tab=requestsinfo · 350
ref-{caseId}financeA refund requested and not moved"{case no.} · refund to move" / "₹{amount} · asked {n}d ago"#/refunds — no such route exists; it redirects home (not in the model yet — to build)warn if > 2 d else info · 620
grnfinanceGRNs without financeSeenOn"{n} goods-received note(s) to pick up" / "each one with its PO attached, for GST filing and reconciliation"#/grninfo · 380
sto-{transferId}warehouseSTO shipped > 7 days ago, not closed (first 8)"{STO no.} · open {n}d" / "{qty} pcs shipped and never counted in — this is the gap ghost stock lives in"#/warehousewarn · 560

Post-processing: any design or planning line whose href starts with #/concepts, #/requirements or #/requests is rewritten to #/design?open=. The "Messages" pane is mentionsFor — every comment that @mentions the person or their role, newest first, with a resolved flag per person.

Rules the earlier text listed that the model does not compute (to build): "new ask · find a vendor" on day 0 (the model waits 3 days), reminders at 5 and 2 days before a hold lapses (the model shows one line whose level changes), "send the PO to the mill" after Push PO, "cloth coming" for the warehouse, "cloth in" for design, "swatches on the way" for the store, "designs to name and price", "PP go-ahead overdue" (3 days), "materials failed check", "milestone slipped", "piece missed the vehicle / turned back" for the merchandiser, "put it in a drop", "display pieces to allocate / confirm display received", "reorder point crossed · international" (the fabric master shows it as a pill and button instead), "mill says out · still on sale" (a Note on the fabric record), "book short of plan" (a tile on Swatch books), URGENT REPLENISH / SIZE FILL / REINTRODUCE (the Stock health intent filter), "case waiting on you" for production / warehouse / finance, "STO arrived, not counted in" for the store at 24 h, "extra fabric to approve / to send", "sheet changed · read it / tell the factory" (the Changed sheets tab and My factory's Changes tab carry these as counts), grouping by rule above 20 lines, per-line age (the pane shows it).

Sources: src/domain/engines.ts (alertsFor, mentionsFor, demandFromStores, returnStatusOf, storeInboundOf); src/screens/Mentions.tsx (MentionBar: todo = alertsFor(db, myRole, { storeId, all: false }), panes "To act on" and "Messages"); src/App.tsx (no /refunds route).

B15. Integrations

Enigma is read, and written only where the API is confirmed; everything else is recorded on the record and printed from it. The working model has no live integration at all: it runs on a seeded database in the browser (realdata.ts generated from the data files, seed.ts for the rest), files in IndexedDB, exports to .xlsx, and window.print. Each row below says what the model stands in with today.

SystemReadWriteOnly recorded (a person does it there)Model today / notes
Enigma — Fabric master (/fabric/v2/…)Code, name, category, inventoryType, vendor, vendorCode, isOnHold, isAutoPo, attributes (Swatch Book, Composition family, Big-Checked, Mill Country, Minimum order quantity, Cost per Unit, Consumed_In, Panna, Thread Count, Weave, Pattern …)isOnHold on pause / resume / supplier switch-off, if grantedDatasheet creation, attribute edits (enigma.configuredOn, ConceptDesign.enigmaOn are recorded, not replicated)Fabric rows seeded; a new cloth gets a stand-in code (NO…-16nn) until Enigma mints one. Full read is one 3.2 MB call; only CREATED_ON is filterable; no updatedAfter; poll nightly plus on demand (not in the model yet — to build)
Enigma — Fabric Inventoryphysical, accounting (reserved), threshold, stored saleable per warehouse—Threshold editsstockMetres / reservedMetres / thresholdMetres; the tool recomputes saleable and uses Enigma's as a cross-check (it drifts)
Enigma — Ledger (/inventory/deductionReport)Every movement with stockBalance, orderProductId, quantity, purposeType, reason——weeklySalesShirts (12 weeks), consumptionM; the source of ROS and consumption. Needs a sku per call — the date/id range is Phase 0 ask N10
Enigma — Fabric PO (/purchase_order/v2)Number, status (DRAFT / Open / Cancelled / Close), vendor, metres, createdAt, overseerId ("1" = auto batch)Create as DRAFT then Open, if grantedRaising the PO in Enigma; the tool records the numberFabricPo with `source: 'auto'
Enigma — Orders and order sheet (/order-module/…, E1 Ordersheet)Order lines, 59 states, statusHistory, sheet fields, ship-by, qrCodeUrl, sizing fieldsOrder-sheet corrections via the Alterations module, if grantedPunching the order (Shopify + Enigma)CustomerOrder mirrors the sheet (spec, measurements, pattern) and logs revisions; stages map to Enigma's scans (MTM_STAGES[].scan); bill number is the tool's
Enigma — Vendors, Warehouses, SizingVendor list (no lead time / MOQ / terms), store and warehouse ids, size sets (/sizing/v4/…)—Vendor creationVendor seeded with leadDays, moq, blockWindowDays typed; SIZE_SETS per category (to confirm against Sizing)
Enigma — GRN (PO fulfillments), STO (/stock-transfer/*, 403 today)GRNs (no number, no invoice), STO statusGRN and STO creation, if grantedElse keyed in Enigma; the tool records the numberGrn minted per inward (GRN-26-nnnn); StockTransfer seeded from the tracker; STO read-back blocked on RBAC
Enigma — RTW variants (/design/list-variants-with-inventory)Variant stock per warehouse (variantSkuCode BOM-SHI-RE-6797-OLIVE-36)——RtwOption.stockBySize is the total; per-store split is storeStockOf (a stable hash) until this is read (to build)
ShopifySales by product code (what is selling), customer profile by name—Listing, price, the manual stock-push buttonInsights runs on seeded weekly sales (realanalytics.ts); ConceptDesign.code is the join; metafield mapping to confirm
ShipHeroFinished-piece stock by location (15 of 50 stores)—PicksReplaces storeStockOf where mapped (to build)
Mill stock lists (email / WhatsApp)Article · metres · price——importMillSheet (paste, one line per fabric), saveMillStock, reportMillStock — the "five emails a morning" typed once
Email—Optional: the PO to the mill, the FOB nomination, the launch PDF, the approval, from the record with the date stampedToday recorded as "emailed on" (fobNomination.emailedOn, coreBlock.via, Block.via)Decide per flow (to build)
Slack / WhatsApp—Optional: post the bell line to a channel—Never the record of truth; the model replaces the threads with Thread + @mentions
Print—Sticker page (one box per design), outbound cut sheet, the factory's day—window.print(); job card, style sheet, BOM, GRN, round-trip slip on screen only (print styles to build)
Export—.xlsx of any visible list (downloadXlsx): fabric-sourcing, rtw-production-tracker, hemming-bench, rtw-stock-health, otif-by-factory, otif-by-order, products-on-sale, dsr, stylist-kpi—Built
Sheets (import once)Fabric ordering, Merchandise Master, REVISED NAME, STO & Swatches Tracker, Monthly Drops, Option Count, ROS & Health / RTW_Shirts_Concise, Replenishment, Trims Working, COGS, Product Weekly Report——realdata.ts is the generated seed from these files; one-time import of open items at go-live; sheets kept in parallel one month

Enigma technical notes for the integration. OAuth2 bearer via /oauth/token with refresh; versioned paths (/fabric/v2/…, /purchase_order/v2, /sizing/v3|v4/…); three timestamp formats in one API (ISO+0000; epoch ms including createdAtIstTime, which is not IST; naive local ISO with microseconds on fulfillments); numeric attributes typed STRING; money carries float artefacts (₹16043.499999999998); soft delete via active, isOnHold, isDeleted, fulfillment status; audit uneven (fabric master returns none; purchaseOrderHistoryList empty); RBAC gates every module (36 of 115 on the discovery account; 403 on stock transfers, bin master, store master, ShipHero orders, allocation engine, location history, notification logs); a webhook module exists with CRUD but no screen; no sandbox; DRAFT PO status exists but 0 of the last 1,000 used it.

Phase 0 checkpoints. A dedicated read-only service account with the seven blocked modules; updatedAfter on fabric / order / inventory lists (N9); date or id range on the ledger (N10); write scope per module; the five human answers (auto-PO quantity rule and whether 08:00 / 15:00 are one job; why 430 of 541 core fabrics have auto-PO off and what the run does with 1,762 vendor-less fabrics; what releases a reservation on cancel / QC-fail; which of the 59 states are final per factory; whether trims exist in Enigma and whether a non-production environment exists).

Sources: docs/Enigma Answers — Read From Live System (26 Aug 2026).md; docs/Enigma Questions — Answered, house format (26 Aug 2026).md; docs/Enigma Questions — for the Enigma team (25 Aug 2026).md; src/domain/engines.ts (storeStockOf, importMillSheet parsing in src/store.ts); src/domain/files.ts (IndexedDB); src/ui.tsx (downloadXlsx, ExportButton); src/domain/realdata.ts, realanalytics.ts (generated seeds).

B17. Non-functional requirements

AreaRequirement (what the model does today, and what is to build)
AuditEvery write on a ProductionOrder appends to its activity[] { at, who, what }; order sheets keep revisions[] with { field, from, to }, reprint and the role; concepts and options keep who chose, rejected or withdrew and why; fabrics keep nameHistory; supply reviews, blocks, plans (forcedBy) and GRNs carry by. Nothing is hard-deleted except a fabric quote or a design (to build: an event for those). A generic per-record activity log for every collection is to build.
UndoThe last 10 writes are on an undo stack (touch() snapshots the whole database); a toast with Undo shows for 7 seconds and may only revert its own change ("Something else changed since — undo from the record instead."). After that a compensating action with a note.
IdentityOne login per team in the prototype (PEOPLE, one person per role; no finance person). Target: one login per person, one role, and for store and factory roles one scope; an administrator role sees everything (walkthrough mode today).
Persistence and concurrencyThe prototype persists one JSON document in localStorage (bsc-plm-v1, version 96; a version bump reseeds). Target: server-side records with optimistic versioning; a stale write is refused with the current state shown; Enigma's reservation is the arbiter when two stores punch the last metres.
ClockThe prototype's clock is fixed at 12 Sep 2026 09:00 IST (TODAY); writes advance from there in real time. Target: real time, IST.
Printingwindow.print() on the sticker page, the outbound cut sheet and the factory's day; print styles for the job card, style sheet, BOM, GRN and the round-trip slip are to build (A4; sticker label stock size to confirm).
FilesAttachments per record with a label from FILE_LABELS per kind and an optional step; bytes in IndexedDB in the prototype (files.ts), stored: false on seeded rows; target: object storage, 25 MB per file, images previewed inline.
Search⌘K palette over bulk POs, MTM and RTW orders, fabrics, RTW options, mills, factories, concepts, returns, launches, customers, stores; results grouped by kind; opens the record.
ListsPaged at 20 (usePager); worked-example strips outside quiet mode; every list exports .xlsx; filters and the open record live in the URL; saved views in the sidebar. Target: the fabric master (~8,600 rows) and orders (~40 a day) filtered server-side.
BellalertsFor runs on every render over the whole database; target: under a second on a server, recomputed on every write to the record (a nightly job is only needed for the rules marked to build in B14).
AvailabilityStores and the warehouse work 10:00–22:00 IST seven days; maintenance windows outside those hours.
DevicesDesktop layout (sidebar 264 px + main up to 1280 px). A narrow layout for the warehouse receiving screens, the swatch desk, the store's returns bench and the hemming hops, with large buttons and a scan field, is to build.
OfflineNone; the store's punch form should warn and block when offline (to build).
Data migrationrealdata.ts is generated from the data files and judged column by column; at go-live a one-time import of open items from the sheets in the Data sources inventory, idempotent, logging every skipped row with a reason; closed history stays in the sheets; Enigma is read, not migrated.
EnvironmentsDevelopment, staging (Enigma read-only against production data), production; Enigma has no sandbox, so writes are enabled per module per environment behind flags.
BackupsDaily, retained 90 days; a record's full history exportable as JSON (Reset demo data reseeds the prototype).
LocalisationEnglish; dates as 12 Sep 26 (en-IN, fmtDate); rupees with Indian grouping (fmtMoney); inches for measurements; metres (or kg for knits) for cloth; relative times "14 minutes ago" on the bell.
AccessibilityKeyboard-operable lists and forms; focus states; contrast AA; the sidebar groups are buttons with aria-expanded.
ObservabilityEvery refused action and every integration call logged with correlation ids; a daily digest of failed Enigma calls to the administrator (to build).
ModesWalkthrough mode (super admin for demos) and Quiet mode (no ledes, no ⓘ, no example strips) are prototype settings; keep Quiet as the pilot default.

Sources: src/store.ts (touch, undoStack, undoTo, persist options, BOOT/now); src/domain/engines.ts (TODAY, fmtDate, fmtMoney, fmtAgo); src/domain/files.ts; src/ui.tsx (usePager, downloadXlsx, useExamples); src/layout/Shell.tsx (layout, views, modes); src/screens/Search.tsx; docs/Enigma Answers — Read From Live System (26 Aug 2026).md (no sandbox).

B18. Implementation plan — phased by team, built on Enigma

This is the order to build in, not the order the process runs in. Enigma stays the system of record throughout — every phase below reads from it, and only moves to writing into it where BSC and the Enigma team have agreed access. The plan is deliberately high-level: which team gets a working screen, roughly what it lets them do, and why that team goes when it does. The exact fields, click paths and edge cases are for the technical discovery that follows this document, working from the detail already laid out flow by flow above.

Before phase 1: a short foundations step — connect read-only to Enigma, set up one login per team and per store or factory, and build the shared page shell (the record view, the notification bell, search, printing) that every later phase reuses. Nothing user-facing ships from this step on its own.

Phase 1 — MTM production and the factory merchandiser, built together. This is the first team-facing release, because it is where the floor's day is most painfully manual today: a planner working from memory and a WhatsApp thread per factory, and no way for a factory to ask for more cloth without a phone call. Bespoke orders ride the same plan: a customer's bespoke shirt waits outside the plan until its cut length has arrived from the mill, then runs like any other made-to-measure order.

  • For the production planner: the daily allocation screen. It proposes which factory each punched order should go to, based on each factory's spare capacity for the day, and lets the planner accept the proposal, override it, or send a batch to a chosen factory. This is the piece the planner uses every morning.

  • For the factory merchandiser, at the same time: their own screen to see what has been sent to their factory, to log the day's output, and to ask for extra cloth when a piece is spoiled — replacing the daily WhatsApp and email.

  • On the production side, alongside the merchandiser's screen: approving an extra-cloth request, which deducts the metres from stock automatically the moment it is approved, instead of someone adjusting Enigma by hand later.

  • Also in this phase: the vehicle round (what came back from each factory today, matched against what was declared) and the running view of how many days a piece has sat in a factory.

Done when a planner and every MTM factory's merchandiser are working from these screens for real orders, and the WhatsApp thread for allocation and extra cloth is no longer the record of what happened.

Phase 2 — Store manager. Bills with more than one piece, a case raised on a delivered piece with the six-rung ladder (alter it here, swap from the shelf, send it to the factory or the hemming unit, refit, remake, or give the money back), store credit, and the store's own view of parcels coming in, stock asks to other stores, and damaged stock write-offs. A bespoke fabric can be picked only while the mill's last report says it is available. This is store-facing and customer-facing, so it goes after the MTM floor has proven the pattern.

Done when a handful of pilot stores are running returns and alterations through this instead of Slack, and store credit has one ledger instead of living in someone's memory.

Phase 3 — Warehouse. Every receipt — fabric rolls, ready-to-wear bulk, bespoke parcels, the vehicle round from MTM factories — writes one numbered goods-received note, always against the purchase order or parcel it belongs to. This phase also covers stock transfers to stores, swatch books and fit samples, and the round trip through the hemming unit. Bespoke lives here too: the mill's daily list typed or pasted in (a fabric reported out goes on hold everywhere at once), the call to the mill with BSC's parcel codes, and the parcel inwarded against its order. It goes after the store phase because by then both sides of most warehouse hand-offs (the factory feeding it, the store receiving from it) are already live.

Done when a fabric receipt updates saleable stock everywhere within the day, and a stock transfer that has sat unclosed for a week shows up as a problem on the warehouse's own list, not as ghost stock nobody notices.

Phase 4 — Design and sourcing. One list for every kind of ask — a new MTM fabric, several fabric options at once, a ready-to-wear style — moving through the same stages: raised, one vendor found and its terms agreed, the purchase order pushed, then downstream. A ready-to-wear style carries its bill of materials and tech pack before it goes to sourcing, with the fabric line as one cloth or a shortlist sourcing picks from; a made-to-measure fabric's sample and department approval are optional, skipped with a reason. This also covers the swatch desk (the six steps from cloth arriving to stores being told) and settling several options from one request. It comes after the three phases above because design and sourcing can keep working over email and Enigma directly in the meantime, whereas the floor teams in phases 1–3 have no such fallback.

Done when ten fabrics run from raised to stores told without the swatch tracker sheet or a hand-made sticker.

Phase 5 — Replenishment and the sales review. The reorder rule for MTM core fabrics (so nobody has to remember to check a mill), the nine-word action list for every ready-to-wear style (reorder it, pull it back, leave it, and so on), and the weekly sales review, daily sales report and stylist scorecard read from one page instead of rebuilt by hand each week. Bespoke has no reorder point — the mill's list is its only availability — so this phase only reads it (the bespoke tab of the weekly review).

Done when the international-fabric spreadsheet and the weekly stock-health scan are retired.

Phase 6 — RTW production. The equivalent for ready-to-wear: one record per style in four sections, as Nitin's team runs it. The request from design. Factory finalisation, where Nitin proposes a factory, cost and date, and design accepts or rejects with a comment and raises the factory PO with the job card, trim card, BOM and tech pack. Fabric finalisation, which is sourcing's one-vendor loop: the fabric PO mints the Enigma code and fills the tracker's fabric details, the cloth goes to the factory, and a short delivery gets a modified ratio or a cut short. Then the milestones and final steps, kept by Nitin and the factory merchandiser from the merchandiser's own portal. A style shows on the tracker from the request, marked "no PO yet". This covers the prototype on a stand-in fabric before any cloth is sourced, the sample rounds, the other BOM items checked against stock and signed off by sourcing after the fabric PO, materials issued to the factory, the day-by-day milestones (cutting, stitching, finishing, dispatch), quality checks before a lot ships, and receiving goods in lots against the order. It comes after design, sourcing and replenishment because half of every tracker row is written by planning, design and sourcing before production touches it, and every row is born from a requirement and carries its fields through; planning and category set the requirement, design the style and fabric specification, sourcing the vendor and the fabric PO, Nitin the factory proposal, the factory merchandiser the receipt of cloth and trims, the production unit and station merchandiser the revised and actual dates and escalations, and the warehouse's receipt closes the row. Production and the warehouse work from this together. It is the tracker sheet as the team reads it today — PO, style, vendor, merchant, quantity, where it is, its date calendar — with every step below hanging off a row on it, and each row born from design's requirement. The workbook's Config and Auto Plan Logic tabs become the tracker's settings (days from PO per milestone per category, risk thresholds, in-full tolerance; saving re-plots open POs' planned dates), and its other four tabs — OTIF dashboard, daily monitor, escalation log, merchandiser alerts — become one analytics screen for the RTW planner, read off the tracker.

Done when one month's cohort of ready-to-wear styles is tracked end to end on the tracker, and the old production spreadsheet is retired for that cohort.

Phase 7 — Writing into Enigma, and the remaining edges. Wherever Enigma access has been agreed by then: purchase orders, fabric on-hold flags, goods-received notes and stock transfers get written back into Enigma instead of only being read from and recorded alongside it. This phase also picks up whatever was deliberately left for later in the phases above — smaller screens sized for a phone, bulk actions, and the handful of rules and alerts flagged "later" throughout this document.

Running two systems side by side. For every phase, the spreadsheet or habit it replaces stays available, read-only, for one month after go-live. Each week, someone compares the new tool against the old sheet with the team that owns it, before that sheet is finally switched off.

Sources: this document's flow-by-flow sections (B5–B12c) for what each phase builds; PRD — First Pivot Plan & Dates v3.0 (19 Aug 2026) and the MTM First Cut thinking document (MTM first, one phase closing before the next opens); Discovery Close-Out — 4-Day Field Plan (07 Sep 2026).

B20. Open questions and assumptions

Assumptions the build stands on until the Bombay follow-ups and the Enigma API review say otherwise. Each changes a field, a source or an owner above. Constants in the model that are placed rather than sourced are flagged in reference.ts comments and repeated here.

Assumed, to confirm.

  • Product-code format and who mints it (BOM-SHI-FM/CS-nnnn, sequence from 4200, minted when a design is added; the model lets design, sourcing and the warehouse add designs).

  • The MTM assortment target of about 30 active options (MTM_ACTIVE_TARGET), the same for every book; the per-family RANGES targets (Poplin 20, Twill 18, Oxford 12, Dobby 8, Linen 14, Stripes 30, Checks 24, Print 10) are placed, not from a sheet.

  • The warehouse, not the factory, inwards bulk cloth for swatches; the swatch desk's six warehouse steps may be pressed in any order.

  • The sticker is a page of boxes, one per design; label stock size.

  • One live vendor per ask at a time (a second add replaces the first, kept as "replaced by sourcing").

  • Go live for stores is design's decision at any moment, not gated on the swatch desk; online follows about two weeks later and is not gated on stores in the model.

  • The factory is proposed by Nitin and accepted by design (no capacity allocation by planning); RQC sits inside the production unit; who exactly is RQC.

  • Store credit has no ceiling; the amount defaults to the piece's MRP (fallback ₹2,990); refunds always go to finance; where store credit lives today (Shopify gift card, Enigma, nowhere).

  • The hemming unit can ship straight to a customer; which stores have a tailor who hems; the return SLA of 10 days.

  • Enigma's per-warehouse threshold (7/3/2) is the store floor and the "Minimum order quantity" attribute is the vendor MOQ; the ROP is max(lead-time demand, MOQ) with no growth uplift for MTM core (the 50% uplift applies to RTW only); 2.15 m per shirt (METRES_PER_SHIRT) and whether pattern uplift is inside it (two screens implied 1.35 m).

  • International is derivable from Mill Country; promotion to core sets a 40 m threshold floor when under 20.

  • Picario: what is sent there and why it is a destination column on the swatch tracker.

  • MTM planning constants: 2-hour freeze, 7-day horizon, 12 pieces per tailor per day, 5-day aging, Mahendra as the corporate factory and Richman as the urgent reserve, dailyTarget 300 / 60 / 60 / 90 / 40 (from the walk, to confirm).

  • RTW constants: MOQ 150 per style, 90-day reorder trigger, 45 / 105 days target cover, WOF 30 / 55, mid sizes 40 / 41 / 42 / 32 / 34 / M / L, ±3% in-full tolerance, 7 / 10-day risk buffers, 35 days fabric-before-inhouse.

  • Costing defaults (trims & packaging ₹44.35, transport 10, hemming 9, shipping 132, overheads 103.6, multiple 3.5) and the per-item trim costs in TRIM_MASTER (placed so a shirt lands on ₹44.35).

  • The fit-sample set: Slim / Standard / Heavy × 38–44 (sizes placed); the three MTM fits sold (Tailored / Classic / Slim / Saved fit).

  • The three placeholder shirt presets (Weekend, Resort, Evening) and the order-sheet pick lists beyond what the BSC-1002370 screenshot showed.

  • Which of the 22 sheets can be retired on day one of the pilot and which run in parallel for a month.

  • Name clashes to settle: Nitin (factory merchandiser at Mahendra in the model; warehouse dispatch in Monthly Drops); "Ramesh" (a driver placeholder in the model; not in any discovery note).

Unknown, blocks a phase.

  • Enigma write access (PO, on-hold, GRN, STO, order sheet) — blocks phase 5 and decides how much stays "recorded".

  • An updatedAfter filter or a change feed — decides polling cost (the full fabric read is 3.2 MB).

  • A date or id range on the ledger — decides whether ROS and consumption can be read at all without 8,585 calls.

  • Whether Shopify sales can be read per product code daily — decides B11's ROS and the What is selling screen.

  • The size-set source (Enigma Sizing module vs SIZE_SETS per category).

  • RTW per-store stock: Enigma variants per warehouse or ShipHero (15 of 50 stores) — until read, storeStockOf is a hash split.

  • The five human answers on the auto-PO (quantity rule; one job or two; why 430 of 541 core fabrics are off; the 1,762 vendor-less fabrics; what releases a reservation on cancel or QC-fail).

Gaps in the model found while verifying this document (to build or decide).

  • Finance has no person, no home screen and no route for "refund to move" (#/refunds); moveRefund has no button.

  • Block.status = 'expired', advanceReturn, advanceOrder, updateEscalation, receiveFabricGrn, conceptChooseFabric / conceptChooseFabricOption exist in the store with no screen or job that calls them.

  • A lapsed hold, an open major sample comment, a missing reason, over-capacity allocation, a second case on a piece, a launch without a name, a size breakup that does not sum: none of these block today (B13 marks each).

  • Only ProductionOrder has an activity[]; the other records rely on their typed history.

Deliberately out of scope. Costing, margin, working capital, ageing and inventory value (the COGS section on a PO stays); customer marketing (Superfone); store checklists (OPSR); the visualiser for MTM styling; store-level replenishment allocation (A7.7) until phase 5; escalations as a workflow.
